# rtoskit-libc — Master Plan

This is the source of truth for the project's goals, architecture and decisions.
The work items live in the phase files:

- [phase-1.md](phase-1.md) — compliant implementation
- [phase-2.md](phase-2.md) — size optimization pass
- [phase-3.md](phase-3.md) — performance optimization pass
- [phase-4.md](phase-4.md) — comparison with Arm Optimized Routines (size, speed, accuracy)
- [phase-5.md](phase-5.md) — speed follow-ups unblocked by the budget (D111; Q16, Q21, Q25)
- [phase-6.md](phase-6.md) — every remaining Arm architecture: M, R and A profiles, AArch32 and AArch64 (D118, [issue #90](https://github.com/sgstreet/rtoskit-libc/issues/90))
- [phase-7.md](phase-7.md) — the minimal POSIX subset for pi-pico and a hosted libstdc++ (D128, [issue #106](https://github.com/sgstreet/rtoskit-libc/issues/106))
- [phase-8.md](phase-8.md) — faster sine and cosine: one reduction for `sincos`, fixed-point double `sin`/`cos` on the soft-double cores (D133)
- [phase-9.md](phase-9.md) — trigonometric follow-ups: `UMULL` for armv7-m's float functions, float `sinf`/`cosf` and `tan`/`tanf` through the D136 kernel (Q41–Q43, D139)
- [phase-10.md](phase-10.md) — POSIX threads in the library, and `<threads.h>` over them (issue #126, D148)
- [phase-11.md](phase-11.md) — the `fast` build type: libm with relaxed accuracy and rounding, against AOR and picolibc (D162)

The crosstool-NG multilib toolchain integration (formerly Phase 5) is tracked as an enhancement, [issue #65](https://github.com/sgstreet/rtoskit-libc/issues/65) (D96).

Status legend used in all phase files: `[ ]` todo, `[~]` in progress, `[x]` done,
`[-]` dropped (with a reason).

---

## 1. Goals

1. A clean-room, ISO/IEC 9899:2024 (C23, N3220) compliant C standard library for
   deeply embedded systems. The normative reference is `docs/standard/library-7.pdf`
   (clause 7) and `docs/standard/annex-b.pdf` (library summary).
2. Implemented in C23 (`-std=c23`). All per-thread library state uses
   `thread_local` (`errno`, `strtok`, `rand`, the `asctime`/`gmtime`/`localtime`
   static buffers, `tmpnam`, ...).
3. A single, explicit **RTOS seam** (`__rtos_*` functions) through which the
   library gets locks, threads, the TLS setup, time, I/O, the heap and process
   exit. No other OS dependency.
4. Minimal conditional compilation. Variation (arch, port, feature flavours) is
   chosen by the **build system selecting files** and by **link-time
   binding**, not by `#if`.
5. The in-tree conformance tests (`test/unit`, D126) validate every step of
   every phase. No step is done until they pass on every enabled
   architecture. (Until D126, musl's libc-test was this gate.)
6. Built only with the build system in `tools/`.

### Phases

| Phase | Goal | Exit criterion |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Compliant implementation | Every applicable libc-test test passes on x86_64 and all ARM targets. The header audit against Annex B is clean. |
| 2 | Size pass | The size baseline is reduced per target, and libc-test still passes in full. |
| 3 | Performance pass | The benchmark baseline is improved per target, libc-test still passes in full, and the size budget from Phase 2 holds. |
| 4 | Arm Optimized Routines comparison | rtoskit-libc is measured against Arm Optimized Routines (`string`, `math`, `fp`; https://github.com/ARM-software/optimized-routines) on every Arm board: code size, instruction counts and accuracy in one report, with the gaps it finds recorded as open questions or later steps. libc-test still passes in full and both size budgets hold. |
| 5 | Speed follow-ups | The speed gaps the per-image budget blocked are closed (Q16, Q21, Q25), libc-test still passes in full and both size budgets hold (D111). |
| 6 | Every Arm architecture | Every Arm architecture in scope (D118) builds in all three build types and passes `make check` against its recorded baseline, with size and speed baselines and budgets recorded. |
| 7 | Minimal POSIX subset | The D128 subset is declared and implemented with `test/unit` cases, `make check` passes on every architecture, pi-pico builds against the library, and a hosted libstdc++ configures and builds against it, with `std::stacktrace` capturing frames. Both size budgets hold for images that do not use the subset. |
| 8 | Faster sine and cosine | `sincos` and `sincosf` reduce their argument once, and double `sin` and `cos` run in fixed point on the cores without a double FPU, with the libm vectors passing at the existing bounds, `make check` passing on every architecture and both size budgets holding. |
| 9 | Trigonometric follow-ups | Q41–Q43 answered on the soft-double cores' speed flavour: armv7-m's float fixed-point functions multiply by `UMULL`, `sinf`/`cosf`/`sincosf` and `tan`/`tanf` take the D136 kernel, every result correctly rounded on the fast path, `make check` passing everywhere and both size budgets holding. |
| 10 | POSIX threads | `<pthread.h>` (D148's scope) is in `libc.a` over the seam, `<threads.h>` is a layer over it, `test/unit/pthread` passes on every architecture and on `port/none`, `test/unit/threads` passes unchanged, and both size budgets hold for programs that use neither API. |
| 11 | The fast build type | A fourth build type, `fast`, builds and passes `make check` on every architecture with the libm vectors at its documented bounds (D162), the other three build types unchanged; on armv7e-m its maths throughput is at or below picolibc's, and on every Arm core at or below AOR's for every function AOR implements; its size budget holds. |

### Scope (decided)

**In scope (ISO C23 clause 7):** `<assert.h>` `<complex.h>` `<ctype.h>`
`<errno.h>` `<fenv.h>` `<float.h>` `<inttypes.h>` `<iso646.h>` `<limits.h>`
`<locale.h>` `<math.h>` `<setjmp.h>` `<signal.h>` `<stdalign.h>` `<stdarg.h>`
`<stdatomic.h>` `<stdbit.h>` `<stdbool.h>` `<stdckdint.h>` `<stddef.h>`
`<stdint.h>` `<stdio.h>` `<stdlib.h>` `<stdnoreturn.h>` `<string.h>`
`<tgmath.h>` `<threads.h>` `<time.h>`

**Out of scope:** `<wchar.h>`, `<wctype.h>`, `<uchar.h>`, POSIX extensions (except the minimal subset of D128),
Annex K (bounds-checking interfaces), decimal floating point (`_Decimal*`, an
optional feature: `__STDC_IEC_60559_DFP__` is not defined).
The multibyte functions that `<stdlib.h>` itself declares (`mblen`, `mbtowc`,
`wctomb`, `mbstowcs`, `wcstombs`) are still required. They are implemented for
the "C" locale only (`MB_CUR_MAX == 1`), and `wchar_t` comes from `<stddef.h>`.

**Order:** libm is implemented **last** in Phase 1.

### Target matrix

| Target | `ARCH_CROSS` (D22) | Test board (`BOARD_TYPE`) | Core / ISA | `long double` | FPU | Role |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `x86_64` | `x86_64` | `linux-x86_64` | x86-64 | 64-bit, see D7 | SSE2 | Phase 1 primary dev/test host and Phase 3 perf reference |
| `armv6-m` | `armv6-m` | `linux-armv6-m` | Cortex-M0+ (Thumb-1) | 64-bit | none (soft) | Phase 2 and 3 target |
| `armv7e-m` | `armv7e-m` | `linux-armv7e-m` | Cortex-M4F (Thumb-2, DSP) | 64-bit | FPv4-SP (hard) | Phase 2 and 3 target |
| `armv7e-m-dp` | `armv7e-m-dp` | `linux-armv7e-m-dp` | Cortex-M7 with the double-precision FPU (Thumb-2, DSP) | 64-bit | FPv5-D16, single + double (hard) | Phase 2 and 3 target |
| `armv8-m.main` | `armv8-m.main` | `linux-armv8-m.main` | Cortex-M33 (Thumb-2, DSP) | 64-bit | FPv5-SP (hard) | Phase 2 and 3 target |

The `linux-*` boards run the test binaries through `port/linux`, natively or
under `qemu-arm`. Real hardware boards (e.g. `pi-pico`, `pi-pico2`) with
`port/none` or `port/rtoskit` can be added as further `BOARD_TYPE` files.
The plan text uses the target names; build commands use the board and
`ARCH_CROSS` names.

The table above is the Phase 1 matrix. `ARCHS` (D106, D108) also builds `armv6s-m` and `armv7-m`, and Phase 6 (D118) adds the remaining Arm architectures: the README's architecture table and `docs/configuration.md` list what is built today.

RISC-V is not targeted for now. The layout allows it to be added as just
another `arch/` and toolchain file.

---

## 2. Decision log

Every decision gets an ID. Revisit a decision by adding a new entry that
supersedes it; do not edit history.

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | **Clean-room implementation.** Other libcs (musl, picolibc, newlib, glibc, Bionic, FreeBSD, ARM optimized-routines, CORE-MATH, ...) are *studied* before writing any algorithm, but no code is copied or mechanically translated. | The user requires clean-room. Keeps the MPL-2.0 licensing unencumbered. |
| D2 | **Research before implementing.** Each non-trivial algorithm gets a note in `docs/research/<topic>.md`: the sources reviewed (with URLs), their trade-offs, the chosen approach, and its edge cases. Notes describe ideas in our own words and contain no copied code. | User requirement. It also records provenance as clean-room evidence. |
| D3 | **libc-test runs natively on a Linux "port".** `port/linux` implements the RTOS seam with raw Linux syscalls (no glibc). On x86_64 the test binaries run natively; ARM binaries run under `qemu-arm` (user mode). | It is fast, scriptable, and gives libc-test the process model it expects. |
| D4 | **The RTOS seam is link-time bound.** The library calls plain external functions `__rtos_*`, declared in `include/rtos/rtos-port.h` (an internal header installed for port authors). Each port supplies them in its own archive (`librtos-<port>.a`). There are no function-pointer tables and no `#ifdef RTOS_X`. Port-defined *types* (e.g. lock storage size) come from `port/<port>/include/rtos/rtos-port-types.h`, found through the include path. | It has zero runtime overhead, needs no conditional compilation, and keeps the seam explicit and auditable. |
| D5 | **Selection by files, not `#if`.** Arch overrides live in `arch/<arch>/src/<area>/<name>.{c,S}`. When an arch provides a file with the same stem as a generic `src/<area>/<name>.c`, the build uses the arch file instead. Arch-specific headers are found through `-I arch/<arch>/include` placed before `-I include`. | This is the user requirement to minimize conditional compilation. |
| D6 | **One external function per source file** (musl-style). Each `thread_local` state object is its own variable next to its only user, not a big shared TLS struct. | Archive members then pull in only what is used. With `-fdata-sections` unused `.tbss`/`.tdata` is dropped, so threads pay TLS only for features the image uses. It also makes arch overrides (D5) trivial. |
| D7 | *Proposed, needs confirmation:* build the x86_64 test configuration with **`-mlong-double-64`**, so `long double` matches the ARM targets (binary64). Everything, including libc-test, is compiled with the same flag and statically linked. | It avoids writing and maintaining a whole ld80 libm and printf/strtold path that no product target uses. libc-test's math tables select by `LDBL_MANT_DIG`, so the binary64 tables are used. If rejected, Phase 1 grows ld80 variants under `src/math/ld80/`. |
| D8 | **The library owns all headers**, including the freestanding ones (`<stddef.h>`, `<stdint.h>`, `<limits.h>`, `<float.h>`, `<stdarg.h>`, `<stdatomic.h>`, ...). They are built on compiler predefined macros (`__INT32_TYPE__`, `__FLT_MANT_DIG__`, `__builtin_va_list`, `__atomic_*`, ...). The library and its tests compile with `-nostdinc -ffreestanding`. | It gives one consistent header set across GCC and Clang. There is no leakage from host glibc headers. |
| D9 | **Toolchain floor: GCC ≥ 14 or Clang ≥ 19** (the first versions with usable `-std=c23`, `constexpr`, `typeof`, `[[attributes]]`, `nullptr`, `_BitInt`). On the Ubuntu 24.04 container use `apt install gcc-14` (GCC 13 rejects `-std=c23`). For ARM, use the `arm-none-eabi` 15.2 toolchain that `setup.mk` already sketches. | Required for the C23 target. |
| D10 | **The TLS model is static `local-exec`.** libc provides `__libc_tls_size`, `__libc_tls_align` and `__libc_tls_init(void *block)`, which copy `.tdata` and zero `.tbss` from linker symbols. The port allocates the per-thread block and sets the thread pointer (x86_64 `%fs`; ARM `__aeabi_read_tp`, provided by the port). | There is no dynamic loader on embedded targets. It is the cheapest correct `thread_local`. |
| D11 | **Heap:** libc owns `malloc` (a clean-room allocator, see the research step). It gets memory from `__rtos_heap_region()`/`__rtos_sbrk()` and is protected by a seam lock. An RTOS may replace `malloc` wholesale by linking its own definitions ahead of libc.a. | Deterministic behaviour matters on embedded systems, and replacing the allocator must stay possible. |
| D12 | **Size and speed flavours** are chosen per build without `#if`. Speed-oriented arch files live in `arch/<arch>/speed/...` and size-oriented ones in `arch/<arch>/size/...`. The build picks a flavour directory (`LIBC_FLAVOUR=size|speed`, default `size`). | It reconciles Phase 2 and Phase 3. Needs confirmation (see Q3). |
| D13 | **Feature trimming is link-time.** Expensive optional paths, such as floating point in `printf`/`scanf`, are reached through a symbol that the full variant defines and a small variant stubs out. The small variant lives in a separate archive (`libc-nofloat.a`) linked ahead of `libc.a`. It is never selected with `#if`. | This is the picolibc-style flavour idea, done at link time. It is a Phase 2 item. |
| D14 | **Tracking:** phase files plus a step ID per session. Each step lists its deliverables and its definition of done. Sessions update the checkboxes, the test baselines and the decision log, then commit. | The work spans many sessions. |
| D15 | **Headers are per target triple, not per multilib.** Every multilib of one GCC (e.g. all `arm-none-eabi` M-profile variants) shares one `include/` directory, so `arch/arm/include/` must be valid for all of them. Types such as `jmp_buf` are sized for the largest variant (with the VFP callee-saved registers: s16–s31 for single precision and d8–d15 for double precision are the same 64 bytes); `fenv_t` (FPSCR) is the same for all. Only `arch/<arch>/src/` (code) differs per multilib. | Phase 4 needs it: GCC multilib installs one sysroot `include/` and per-multilib `lib/<multidir>/`. Deciding this early stops Phases 1 to 3 from baking per-arch header differences in. |
| D16 | **The build can be driven from outside.** A caller (crosstool-NG) can build and install one multilib with `make ARCH_CROSS=<arch> CROSS_COMPILE=<prefix> MULTILIB_FLAGS='<flags>' MULTIDIR=<dir> PREFIX=<sysroot> install`, and install headers alone with `make install-headers`. | crosstool-NG needs headers before the final GCC, and then one libc build per multilib. |
| D17 | **Arch code comes from ordered layers.** Each `tools/makefiles/<arch>.mk` sets `ARCH_LAYERS`, an ordered list of `arch/<layer>/src` directories. For each source stem the first layer that has the file wins; otherwise the generic `src/` file is used. Layers are per ISA (`armv6-m`, `armv7e-m`, `armv8-m.main`) and per FPU (`fpv4-sp`, `fpv5-sp`, `fpv5-dp`). For example, `armv7e-m-dp` uses `armv7e-m-dp fpv5-dp fpv5-sp armv7e-m`. This extends D5. | It shares FPU-specific code between ISAs (the Cortex-M7 DP unit and FPv5 on v8-M) and ISA-specific code between FPU variants, without duplicating files and without `#if`. |
| D18 | **Support the Cortex-M double-precision FPU (FPv5-D16, e.g. Cortex-M7 with the DP unit)** as the arch `armv7e-m-dp` (`-march=armv7e-m+fp.dp -mfloat-abi=hard`, i.e. FPv5-D16; D24). On it, `double` operations are hardware; `float` and `double` both get FPv5 instructions (`VSQRT`, `VFMA`, `VRINT[ANPMZX]`, `VMAXNM`/`VMINNM`, `VCVT[ANPM]`) through the `fpv5-sp`/`fpv5-dp` layers. Note that FPv5-SP (Cortex-M33, and the M7 SP option) also has these instructions for `float`, but FPv4-SP (Cortex-M4F) does not. | User requirement. It needs no header changes (D15 holds: the register file and FPSCR are shared). |
| D19 | **Exception to the no-`#if` rule:** `<limits.h>` selects `CHAR_MIN`/`CHAR_MAX` with `#ifdef __CHAR_UNSIGNED__`. Plain `char` signedness is a compiler option with no predefined *value* macro, and the limits must be usable in `#if`. Further compiler-property selections in headers need their own entry. | There is no conditional-free way to express it. |
| D20 | **`<errno.h>` values follow the Linux numbering** (`EDOM` 33, `ERANGE` 34, `EILSEQ` 84, `EBADF` 9, ...). Seam functions return negated errno values, so `port/linux` passes kernel results through unchanged, and other ports map their own codes. | Simplest for the test port. The values are arbitrary otherwise. |
| D21 | **Build layout:** `src/`, `arch/` and `include/` are source pools with no `.mk` files. The `libc/` module compiles them (through `libc/subdir.mk` → `tools/makefiles/libc.mk`), and the `port/<port>/` modules select from their own `src/` and `arch/<layer>/src/` the same way. `BUILD_ROOT` and `INSTALL_ROOT` include `ARCH_CROSS` (`build/<arch>/<type>`, `images/<arch>/<type>`). Consumers (`example/`, `test/`) get the libc include and link flags from `tools/makefiles/libc-target.mk`, which each `<arch>.mk` includes. | It keeps one compile rule for layered selection (D5, D17), and lets several arches coexist in one tree. |
| D22 | **Build system usage follows the pi-pico conventions** (supersedes D5, D17 and D21). (1) Every source directory is a module with its own `<dir>.mk` (from `tools/templates/src-template.mk`) and compiles in place. Library code lives under `libc/<area>/` and `port/<port>/`, and private headers in `libc/internal/` (header-only, no `.mk`). (2) Arch variation lives in variant subdirectories named after the `ARCH_CROSS` toolchain file (`libc/tls/x86_64/`, `port/linux/armv8-m.main/`). The parent module selects them with `SUBDIRS := ${ARCH_CROSS}` (as `sys/sys.mk` does in pi-pico). Code shared between cores goes in a subdirectory named by a variable the toolchain file sets (e.g. `LIBC_FPU := fpv5-dp`, `SUBDIRS := ${ARCH_CROSS} ${LIBC_FPU}`). When a generic C function needs an arch override (Phase 2/3), the generic module's `subdir.mk` filters it out of `SRC`. (3) Archives are built by a parent module that lists its children in `TARGET_OBJ_LIBS` with `all: ${CURDIR}/libX.a` (`libc/libc.mk` → `libc.a`, `port/linux/linux.mk` → `librtos-linux.a`, like pi-pico `runtime.mk`). (4) Executables list the object directories they link in `TARGET_OBJ_LIBS` (e.g. `port/${RTOS_PORT} port/${RTOS_PORT}/${ARCH_CROSS}`). The toolchain file links `libc.a` from the build tree through `LDLIBS`, as pi-pico's toolchain file points at its build-tree picolibc. (5) A `BOARD_TYPE` file (`tools/makefiles/<board>.mk`) exports `ARCH_CROSS` and `RTOS_PORT`. The `ARCH_CROSS` file (`tools/makefiles/<arch>.mk`) holds the toolchain and all flags, including `LIBC_CFLAGS` for library modules. The top-level `Makefile` loops over `BOARDS` × `BUILDS` into `build/<board>/<build-type>`. (6) Builds always run from the project root. | Required by the user: the build system in `tools/` must be used the way pi-pico uses it. |
| D23 | **Tests run with `make check`** (amends D22 (6)): the top-level `Makefile` builds, then runs `test/unit/run-unit` on `${BUILD_ROOT}/test/unit` for every board and build type. libc-test joins the same target in P1.02. There is no `tools/test-runner`. | User direction. |
| D24 | **`ARCH_CROSS` toolchain files and variant subdirectories are named after the architecture, not a core** (amends D22): `x86_64`, `armv6-m`, `armv7e-m`, `armv7e-m-dp`, `armv8-m.main`, with test boards `linux-<arch>`. The ARM toolchain files select the target with `-march` plus extensions (e.g. `armv7e-m+fp`, `armv7e-m+fp.dp`, `armv8-m.main+fp+dsp`) and `-mfloat-abi`, never `-mcpu`. That way the library is not tuned for one core, and the names match the GCC multilib directories used in Phase 4. | User direction. |
| D25 | **Lowercase everywhere unless the standard requires uppercase.** Applies to our identifiers, constants (as `static constexpr`, not macros), include guards (`__<name>_h`), file names (`_Exit` is in `_exit.c`) and test output. The exceptions are standard-mandated names (`_Exit`, `FILE`, the limit macros, `E*` errno macros per C23 7.5p4) and compiler/toolchain names (`__INT32_TYPE__`, `.note.GNU-stack`). This refines D6, which says the file is named after its function. | User direction. |
| D26 | **Linux kernel coding style, as in pi-pico** (supersedes D25 for macros and include guards): lower-case identifiers and file names; `ALL_CAPS` macros and enum constants (`__LINUX_SYS_WRITE`, `__LIBC_TCB_ALIGN`); include guards `_NAME_H` closed with `#endif /* _NAME_H */`; `/* */` comments only; `while (true)`; no alignment padding; no new typedefs beyond those the standard requires; `extern` declarations only in headers. `tools/clang-format` stays authoritative for layout. From D25, the lowercase file names (`_exit.c`) and the lowercase test output remain. | User direction. |
| D27 | **libc-test harness** (P1.02): `test/libc-test/manifest.txt` drives the build; each suite module (`test/libc-test/{api,functional,math,regression}`) includes the shared fragment `tools/makefiles/libc-test.mk`, which builds one ELF per `run` test straight from the submodule sources with our headers, the shim and the port objects. libc-test's warning set applies without `-Werror` (the api tests keep `-pedantic-errors -Werror`). `test/libc-test/shim/include/test.h` replaces libc-test's `test.h` (which needs `<unistd.h>`), and `t_printf` writes through `__rtos_write`. There is no `runtest`: `test/libc-test/run` applies the timeout and `TARGET_RUNNER`, writes `results.txt`, and fails on PASS→non-PASS and on failures missing from `expected/<board>.txt`; `make check LIBC_TEST_UPDATE=1` records improvements only. `api/main` has status `harness`. Executables also relink when `libc.a` changes (`EXTRA_ELF_DEPS` in the toolchain file). | Keeps the tools build system and avoids fork, which ports such as `port/none` cannot provide. |
| D28 | **Formatted-output engine** (P1.02, `docs/research/printf-engine.md`): one internal engine `__libc_format()` writes in chunks to a `struct __libc_format_sink` (buffer sinks now, `FILE` sinks in P1.09); the length modifier is kept as an enum so `va_arg` and `%n` use the exact type; floats go through the single replaceable symbol `__libc_format_float()` (stub until P1.08, D13). No positional `%n$` (POSIX). `%p` prints `0x` plus lowercase hex (`0x0` for null); `%s` of a null pointer prints `(null)`; `%lc`/`%ls` fail with `EILSEQ` (wide characters are out of scope); an unsupported `wN`, an invalid specification or decimal-float modifiers fail with `EINVAL`; results above `INT_MAX` fail with `EOVERFLOW`, checked before each field is written. `EINVAL` (22) and `EOVERFLOW` (75) join `<errno.h>` with Linux values (D20). | Standard text (C23 7.23.6.1) plus the reviewed engines; one engine for every destination keeps the size down. |
| D29 | **Executables declare the library dependency themselves** (amends D27): every executable module sets `EXTRA_ELF_DEPS := ${BUILD_ROOT}/libc/libc.a`, including the libc-test suite modules, instead of the toolchain file appending it. | User direction; keeps toolchain files free of module dependencies. |
| D30 | **String, character and bit utilities** (P1.03, `docs/research/string-ctype-stdbit.md`): word-at-a-time `strlen`/`memchr`/`strchr` (via `__libc_strchrnul`)/`memset` and co-aligned `memcpy`/`memmove`, reading only aligned `uintptr_t` words through `__builtin_memcpy` (`libc/internal/word.h`), byte loops elsewhere until P3.02; Two-Way `strstr`; 256-bit sets for `strspn`/`strcspn`; the C23 const-preserving search macros select with `_Generic(0 ? (p) : (void *)1, ...)` and the library defines those functions with parenthesised names; `strerror` is a `switch`; `<ctype.h>` is table-free and "C"-locale only; `assert` calls `__libc_assert_fail` (reserved-name rule, instead of the plan's `__assert_fail`) through a one-`bool`-parameter helper so `assert()` and `assert(1, 1)` are diagnosed; `<stdbit.h>` generic macros and all 70 typed functions use GCC 14's `__builtin_stdc_*`, `<stdckdint.h>` uses `__builtin_*_overflow`, and the endianness macros come from `__BYTE_ORDER__`. Unit tests build with `-fno-builtin` so they exercise the library rather than compiler folding. | Standard text plus the reviewed libcs; smallest generic code that is correct everywhere, with speed left to P3. |
| D31 | **Allocator and the heap/lock seam** (P1.04, `docs/research/malloc.md`; refines D11): a clean-room TLSF allocator (O(1) `malloc`/`free`, 16 second-level lists, payloads aligned to `2 * sizeof(size_t)`, one word of overhead per used block, control block carved from the largest region, `__libc_malloc_check()` for tests). Heap seam: `bool __rtos_heap_region(index, &base, &size)`, called until false at the first allocation (≤ 8 fixed regions), not `__rtos_sbrk`. Lock seam: opaque port-defined `struct __rtos_lock`, recursive `__rtos_lock_acquire/release`, `__rtos_lock_create/destroy` for run-time locks, and statically allocated locks named by libc and defined by the port (`__rtos_malloc_lock` now), so `libc.a` never depends on a port's lock size (this replaces the port-defined lock type header that D4 sketched). `port/none`: no-op locks and one heap region between the weak `__rtos_none_heap_start`/`__rtos_none_heap_end` linker symbols; `port/linux`: a 64 MiB `MAP_NORESERVE` region and depth-checking single-thread locks until P1.12. `malloc(0)` and `realloc(p, 0)` give a unique minimum block; failures set `ENOMEM` (12). | Deterministic timing and bounded fragmentation suit an RTOS; fixed regions match MCU memory maps; opaque locks keep one library per target. |
| D32 | **Two allocators, chosen at build time** (amends D31; `docs/research/malloc.md`): `libc/malloc/nano` (the default: first fit over an address-ordered free list with merging, written from the design of picolibc's allocator, 838 B of Thumb-2 for the full set on armv6-m) and `libc/malloc/tlsf` (O(1), 1532 B). The board file sets `LIBC_MALLOC ?= nano`; `make LIBC_MALLOC=tlsf` overrides it. Both variants are always built and `libc.a` takes `libc/malloc/${LIBC_MALLOC}` (a variant subdirectory, D22, no `#if`); `libc/malloc` holds the shared `calloc`, `free_sized`, `free_aligned_sized`. `test/unit/malloc` links one executable per variant so `make check` tests both. Changing `LIBC_MALLOC` in an existing build tree needs `make clean`, since archives are updated in place. | The user wants the small allocator by default and a choice; TLSF stays for bounded-time systems. |
| D33 | **`<stdlib.h>` part 2, `<inttypes.h>`, `<setjmp.h>`, `<signal.h>`** (P1.05, `docs/research/stdlib-part2.md`): one `__libc_strtoint()` core for the `strtol`/`strtoimax` family (C23 `0b` prefix, `EINVAL` for a bad base); heapsort `qsort` (smallest, O(n log n) worst case, no recursion; a speed flavour may follow in P3); `bsearch` with the const-preserving generic macro; `rand` is PCG32 with `thread_local` state and `RAND_MAX` 0x7fffffff; `atexit`/`at_quick_exit` use fixed 32-entry tables under the new seam lock `__rtos_atexit_lock`; `exit` runs the handlers then `.fini_array`; new seam functions `__rtos_getenv` and `__rtos_system`; multibyte functions for a single-byte "C" locale mapping bytes to the same wide value; `<inttypes.h>` `PRI`/`SCN` macros built on the C23 `wN`/`wfN` modifiers (`t` for `PTR`, asserted equal to `ptrdiff_t`), so the header has no per-target length letters; `jmp_buf` sized by one expression (`10 * sizeof(void *) + 64` bytes) that fits x86_64 and Arm, x86_64 `setjmp`/`longjmp` in `libc/setjmp/x86_64/`; software `signal`/`raise` with handler reset and default actions `__rtos_abort` (SIGABRT) or `_Exit(128 + sig)`. `call_once`/`once_flag` (also in `<stdlib.h>` in C23) wait for P1.12, `wcstoimax`/`wcstoumax` are out of scope (wide characters). | Standard text plus the reviewed libcs; small code, no `#if`, one header set per triple (D15). |
| D34 | **Shared arch code and board link options** (P1.06, amends D22): besides `ARCH_CROSS`, every toolchain file exports `ARCH_FAMILY` (`x86_64`, `arm`), the target triple's family. Code that is the same for every core of the family lives in a `${ARCH_FAMILY}` variant subdirectory: `port/linux/arm/` (Thumb-1 entry, out-of-line `svc` system calls, `set_tls`, `__aeabi_read_tp`) and `libc/tls/arm/` (variant I); executables link `port/${RTOS_PORT}/${ARCH_FAMILY}`. Code that depends on the core (ISA or FPU) stays in `${ARCH_CROSS}` subdirectories: `libc/setjmp/<arch>/`, `libc/fenv/<arch>/`. The Arm toolchain files also export `LIBC_FPU` (`nofp`, `fpv4-sp`, `fpv5-dp`, `fpv5-sp`) for FPU-shared code (first user: `test/unit/fpv5`; libm from P1.13). The Arm toolchain files share `tools/makefiles/arm-none-eabi.mk`, and a board passes link options through `BOARD_LDFLAGS` (the Linux Arm boards add `port/linux/arm/headers.ld`, which makes ld load the ELF headers so `__ehdr_start` exists). | The four Arm architectures share one Linux ABI and one TLS ABI; copying that code four times would be worse than one more variable. |
| D35 | **`armv6-m` builds with `-march=armv6s-m`** (amends D24): Armv6-M without the S extension has no `SVC`, which GAS rejects; every Cortex-M0/M0+ implements Armv6S-M, `-mcpu=cortex-m0` selects it, and GCC's `thumb/v6-m/nofp` multilib is built for it. The `ARCH_CROSS`, board and multilib names stay `armv6-m`. | Matches the real cores and the multilib exactly. |
| D36 | **The Arm boards run under `qemu-arm -cpu max`** (`TARGET_RUNNER` in `tools/makefiles/linux-<arch>.mk`; `docs/research/arm-bring-up.md`). The Linux port's `__aeabi_read_tp` reads TPIDRURO (CP15), which qemu's M-profile CPU models fault on, so the A-profile `max` model is required; it executes everything the four architectures use, including FPv5-D16 `VRINT`/`VMAXNM`/`VFMA.F64` (checked by `test/unit/fpv5`). Instruction-set conformance comes from the compiler and assembler `-march`, not from qemu. The toolchain is arm-none-eabi GCC 15.2 from `make setup` (D9), installed in `local/`. | The only way to have per-thread TLS in a Linux process under qemu-user; real M-profile ports supply their own `__aeabi_read_tp`. |
| D37 | **`armv6-m` and `armv6s-m` are separate architectures; the Arm toolchain is GCC 16.2** (supersedes D35, amends D36 and D9): `armv6-m.mk` builds with `-march=armv6-m` (no `SVC`) and the new `armv6s-m.mk` with `-march=armv6s-m` (Cortex-M0/M0+, Armv6-M with the OS extension); both use GCC's `thumb/v6-m/nofp` multilib, and each has a `linux-<arch>` board, a baseline and `${ARCH_CROSS}` variant subdirectories (`libc/setjmp/armv6s-m/`, `libc/fenv/armv6s-m/`, the same code as `armv6-m`). The shared Linux port emits `svc #0` as raw bits (`.inst.n 0xdf00`) so it assembles for plain Armv6-M. `make setup` installs arm-none-eabi GCC 16.2 (red-rocket-computing/cross-dev v0.3.0); its `setup.mk` target is the versioned driver, so a version change reinstalls `local/`. GCC 16.2 implements `_BitInt` on Arm, so `test/unit/bitint` builds on every board (Q12 resolved). | User direction: both architectures are targets, and the newer toolchain closes the `_BitInt` gap. |
| D38 | **Decimal and hexadecimal to binary floating point** (P1.07, `docs/research/strtod.md`): one core `__libc_strtofloat()` parameterised by the target format (precision, exponent range, an exact fast-path callback) and the rounding direction (`FLT_ROUNDS`), with `strtod`/`strtof` as thin wrappers, `strtold` = `strtod` (D7) and `atof` = `strtod(nptr, nullptr)`. Clinger's fast path (≤ 19 digits, significand ≤ 2^precision, \|exponent\| ≤ 22 or 10, round-to-nearest only) does one hardware or soft-float operation; everything else goes through Simple Decimal Conversion (800 digits plus a truncated flag, shifts of ≤ 28 bits with 32-bit accumulators, about 820 bytes of stack on that path only) or the hexadecimal reader, and one shared round-and-pack step (`__libc_float_pack`) rounds once in any direction, subnormals included. Eisel–Lemire was rejected for its ≈ 10 KiB table and Gay's `dtoa` for heap use. Overflow and underflow (tiny and inexact) set `ERANGE`; exception flags wait for `<fenv.h>` (P1.13); the NaN payload is ignored. `<math.h>` starts with only `HUGE_VAL*`, `INFINITY` and `NAN`. A faster middle path for short inputs with large exponents is a Phase 3 item. | Correct rounding in every mode with small code and no tables or allocation; speed for rare inputs is left to Phase 3. |
| D39 | **Floating-point formatting** (P1.08, `docs/research/printf-float.md`): `%e %f %g` expand the value exactly into decimal (at most 767 significant digits) with the decimal type from P1.07, now the shared module `libc/decimal/` (`__libc_decimal_set`, `__libc_decimal_shift_left/right`, `__libc_decimal_round`), round once at the requested digit in the `FLT_ROUNDS` direction, and write the digits as pieces, so any precision is exact and needs no buffer; `%g` takes its style from the exponent of the value rounded to P digits, which rounds at the same position, so there is no double rounding. `%a` is formatted from the bits, subnormals normalised to a leading `1` and rounding carries renormalised (`0x1.0p+1`, where glibc prints `0x2.0p+0`; both are allowed). Infinity and NaN print as `inf`/`nan` (uppercase for `AEFG`) with the sign, and the `0` flag does not pad them. `__libc_format_float()` stays the single replaceable symbol (D13) and now receives the running count so `EOVERFLOW` is detected before writing (D28). `strfromd`/`strfromf`/`strfroml` call `snprintf`. Ryū printf (~100 KiB of tables) and shortest-digit algorithms (exact only to 17 digits) were rejected. Cost: ≈ 830 bytes of stack per float conversion for the decimal. | Exact, correctly rounded output in every mode with no tables, reusing the strtod code; the stack cost is the price, revisited in Phase 2. |
| D40 | **`<stdio.h>` streams** (P1.09, `docs/research/stdio.md`): a 36-byte (32-bit) `FILE` with one buffer window used in either direction (reading `[pos, end)`, writing `[buffer, pos)`), a handle, flags, one pushback byte, a lock pointer and list links; no function pointers, the seam's handles are the only backend. Buffers are `malloc(BUFSIZ)` at the first transfer (`BUFSIZ` 256, the C minimum) or come from `setvbuf`; without memory a stream works unbuffered. `stdin`/`stdout` are line buffered and `stderr` unbuffered (interactivity is unknown, so full buffering is not allowed, 7.23.3p7); opened files are fully buffered. A new-line on a line-buffered stream flushes its whole buffer. One character of pushback (a second `ungetc` fails). Locks: per-stream recursive seam locks (created by `fopen`/`tmpfile`; port-named `__rtos_stdin/stdout/stderr_lock` for the standard streams) and `__rtos_stdio_lock` for the open-file list and the `tmpnam`/`tmpfile` counters, always taken list-then-stream; the input functions flush line-buffered output before taking their own lock (7.23.3p3), which is earlier than required but avoids inverting that order. `exit` flushes the standard streams and closes the others through a destructor in `__libc_file_write.c`, which is linked whenever buffered output can exist. The seam's I/O part is final: `__rtos_open` with `__RTOS_OPEN_*` flags (including TEMPORARY, removed when closed), `__rtos_close`, `__rtos_seek` (64-bit offsets), `__rtos_remove`, `__rtos_rename`, `__rtos_tmpname` (the port names temporary files; Linux uses `/tmp/rtoskit-<pid>-<n>`). `freopen` without a file name allows only modes the stream's access already covers. `setvbuf` is refused while the stream holds data and accepted otherwise. `<errno.h>` gains the file-system numbers (Linux values, D20). | RAM per stream was the main concern; one backend keeps the code small; per-stream locks keep a blocked reader from stalling other threads' output. |
| D41 | **The scanf family** (P1.10, `docs/research/scanf.md`): one engine `__libc_scan()` over a string or a locked `FILE`, with one character of pushback. A field reads characters while they still form a prefix of a matching sequence (7.23.6.2p9), so an incomplete number such as `"0x"`, `"1e"` or `"nan(12"` is consumed and is a matching failure, where glibc's `sscanf` accepts the shorter number. A character the engine pushes back into a stream goes back into the read window it came from, keeping the stream's own pushback slot free for the caller's `ungetc`. Floats are scanned into a normal form (up to 900 significant digits plus a sticky digit, the point folded into the exponent) and converted by `strtof`/`strtod`/`strtold`, through the replaceable symbol `__libc_scan_float` (D13). `%[` ranges `a-z` are supported; `%lc`/`%ls`/`%l[` fail with `EILSEQ` (wide characters are out of scope); an invalid conversion stops the scan with `EINVAL`. Integer overflow wraps as in `strtoumax` (undefined behaviour). | Standard text (C23 7.23.6.2) and libc-test; one pushback character is all a stream keeps (D40). |
| D42 | **`<time.h>`, `<locale.h>` and the time seam** (P1.11, `docs/research/time-locale.md`): `time_t` and `clock_t` are `long long` on every target (seconds; microseconds with `CLOCKS_PER_SEC` 1000000), so one header serves every core (D15) and there is no 2038 limit. The seam has `__rtos_clock_gettime(base, ts)` and `__rtos_clock_getres(base, res)` taking the `TIME_*` bases (`TIME_UTC`, `TIME_MONOTONIC`, `TIME_ACTIVE`, `TIME_THREAD_ACTIVE`); `clock` is `TIME_ACTIVE`, which replaces the planned `__rtos_cpu_time`. Calendar conversion uses March-based 400-year eras (no tables, exact for every `int` year); `gmtime` fails with `EOVERFLOW` beyond them. Local time follows the POSIX `TZ` string, read at every conversion (no `tzset` state; Q6): `Jn`, `n` and `Mm.w.d` rules with times from −167 to 167 hours; unset, empty, `:`-prefixed or invalid values are UTC, and DST without rules uses `M3.2.0,M11.1.0`. `mktime` with a negative `tm_isdst` reads gap and overlap times as standard time. `strftime` implements the POSIX `0`/`+` flags and widths for `%C %F %G %Y`, with plain `%Y` as `%+4Y`. Only the "C" locale exists: `setlocale` accepts `"C"`, `"POSIX"` and `""`, and `localeconv` returns one constant structure. `<errno.h>` defines the POSIX error numbers with Linux values (D20) and `strerror` has a message for each. | Standard text (C23 7.11, 7.29), POSIX XBD 8.3; libc-test `functional/strftime` and `functional/time`. |
| D43 | **Threads, atomics and the thread seam** (P1.12, `docs/research/threads.md`; supersedes the native mutex/condition seam sketched in §4): the seam gains `__rtos_thread_create/exit/join/detach/self/yield/sleep` (an opaque `struct __rtos_thread`, so `thrd_t` is a pointer of one size for every port, D15), a futex-like `__rtos_futex_wait(address, expected, TIME_UTC deadline)`/`__rtos_futex_wake(address, count)`, and support for the `__atomic_*` library: a word compare-and-swap `__rtos_atomic_cas` (atomic against inline plain stores) for 1-, 2- and 4-byte operations, and a critical section `__rtos_atomic_enter/exit` for 8-byte and odd-sized ones, which no target inlines. libc writes mutexes (three-state futex word, owner and count for recursion), condition variables (a sequence counter), `call_once` and `tss` (32 keys with generations, `thread_local` values, destructor passes linked through a weak reference from `thrd_exit`, D13) once over these, and provides the `__atomic_*` library functions in `libc/atomic/`, one file per operation with its 1/2/4/8-byte forms (a narrow exception to D6: they are one generated family). `thrd_create` passes the port a trampoline; the last `thrd_exit` calls `exit(EXIT_SUCCESS)`. `<stdatomic.h>` is built on the GCC builtins, scaling pointer operands of `atomic_fetch_add/sub` by the element size. Linux port: `clone` with `CLONE_SETTLS`/`CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID`, one mapping per thread (256 KiB stack, record, TLS block), detached threads reclaimed lazily, futex-based recursive seam locks, `__kuser_cmpxchg` for the Arm compare-and-swap. `port/none`: one thread, `__rtos_none_start(tls_image)` with a static 1 KiB TLS block, interrupt masking for atomics, futex waits that spin. | One small seam any RTOS can implement (a wait queue keyed by address); the synchronisation logic is written and tested once; armv6-m needs atomics that stay correct beside inline stores. |
| D44 | **`<fenv.h>`** (P1.13, `docs/research/fenv-libm.md`): exceptions and directed rounding exist only where every floating type is the FPU's (x86_64 SSE, Arm with a double-precision FPU: `__x86_64__` or `__ARM_FP & 8`); elsewhere (armv6-m, armv6s-m, and the single-precision FPUs, whose double arithmetic is libgcc software that rounds to nearest without flags) only `FE_TONEAREST` is defined and `FE_ALL_EXCEPT` is 0. This is the one `#if` in the header, allowed as a macro the standard defines only when supported; the types and the values are identical for every target (the Arm FPSCR encoding, translated to MXCSR on x86_64), so D15 holds. Implementation variants `libc/fenv/{x86_64,fpv5-dp,soft}` are selected by `LIBC_FENV` in the toolchain file (replacing the per-`ARCH_CROSS` `__flt_rounds` directories); the generic functions use a per-variant `fenv-arch.h`. Unsupported exception bits are ignored. | Conformance: a macro may be defined only if every operation honours it. |
| D45 | **libm accuracy and error reporting** (P1.13): exact operations are exact, and `fma` and the narrowing functions correctly rounded, in every supported direction with IEEE exceptions; later functions aim for correctly rounded float and at most 1 ulp double to nearest. `math_errhandling` is `MATH_ERREXCEPT` (no `errno`); libm raises exceptions by arithmetic (no-ops on cores without them) and is compiled with `-frounding-math -fsignaling-nans -fno-math-errno`. `fma` and the narrowing functions share one exact core (128-bit significand and sticky bit) whose last rounding is a hardware addition for normal results and a manual rounding from `FLT_ROUNDS` for subnormal ones (tininess before rounding). Long double functions call the double ones. | Q-free target for the remaining libm steps; one code path for every core. |
| D46 | **libm in `libc.a`, FPU layers** (P1.13): libm is part of `libc.a`, and an empty `libm.a` is installed so `-lm` links. Instruction versions live in `libc/math/<layer>/` (`fpv4-sp`, `fpv5-sp`, `fpv5-dp`); `LIBC_MATH_LAYERS` in the toolchain file lists a core's layers (armv7e-m-dp has `fpv5-sp fpv5-dp`), and `libc/math/subdir.mk` drops the generic file of the same name (D22). `fmax`/`fmin` follow IEEE 754-2008 `maxNum`/`minNum` so `VMAXNM`/`VMINNM` and the C code agree. | One include set, no `#if`, instructions where the core has them; `test/unit/fpu_math` compares both paths. |
| D47 | **Exponentials, logarithms, powers and roots** (P1.14, `docs/research/libm-exp-log.md`): two table-driven double-double cores (Tang): `__libc_exp_core` (2^(j/64), 64 entries, Taylor to r^7) and `__libc_log_core` (log(1 + j/128), 92 entries, series to f^9), with `__libc_log1p_core`, `__libc_expm1_core` and `__libc_pow_integer` on top, serve every function of the step; each result is rounded once at the end (relative core error about 2^-68), so double is within 1 ulp (0.61 seen) and float, computed in double, correctly rounded to nearest. The cores switch to round-to-nearest (TwoSum and the exact product are exact only there) and restore the caller's direction for the last rounding; a subnormal result is rounded once through a bias of 1 (`__libc_exp_scale`). Tiny arguments go through `__libc_math_tiny`/`__libc_math_tinyf`, which round x or its neighbour in the current direction and raise underflow. Exact cases (powers of two in `pow`, `log2`, `exp2`) raise nothing; elsewhere an undeserved inexact is allowed (F.10). `powr(0, -inf)` is +inf without divide-by-zero (IEEE 754 9.2.1). x86_64 gains the FPU layer `libc/math/x86_64` (`sqrtsd`/`sqrtss`); `vsqrt` joins the Arm layers. | One pair of cores keeps the code small and the accuracy uniform; correct rounding (CORE-MATH) remains the long-term target. |
| D48 | **Trigonometric, pi-scaled and hyperbolic functions** (P1.15, `docs/research/libm-trig.md`): argument reduction is exact for every double: Cody–Waite with pi/2 in 33+33+53+53 bits below 2^20, Payne–Hanek above with 32-bit limbs (no 128-bit integers on Arm), seven words of a 40-word 2/pi table and at least 191 fraction bits. sin/cos kernels are Taylor series on |r| <= pi/4 with the r^3/6 and r^2/2 terms exact as double-doubles; tan is their double-double quotient. One atan2 core (Tang's atan(k/32) table, 33 entries) serves atan, atan2, asin and acos (through sqrt((1-x)(1+x)) as a double-double) and the pi-scaled inverses (times 1/pi as a double-double). sinpi/cospi/tanpi reduce exactly (x - n/2) and return the IEEE 754 9.2.1 zeros, ones and infinities directly, without inexact. Hyperbolic functions use Taylor series below 1 and the D47 exp core above, `__libc_log1p_dd` for the inverses. Results are rounded once in the caller's direction (D47); the float functions nudge tiny arguments by the sign of f(x) - x, and at +-FLT_MIN raise no underflow when the result rounds back to FLT_MIN. Library code avoids double-to-int64 conversions where exactness matters: libgcc's raises inexact on the FPv5-dp core. | One reduction and two kernels keep the code small; double stays within 0.51 ulp in the tests. |
| D49 | **Special functions, `<complex.h>`, `<tgmath.h>`** (P1.16, `docs/research/libm-special.md`, `docs/research/libm-complex.md`): erf/erfc from the Maclaurin series below 1/2, a 57-entry Hermite–Taylor table (erfc(c), 2/sqrt(pi) e^-c^2 at c = 1/2 + k/16) to 4 and the Laplace continued fraction (30 levels, double-double) to 27.3; lgamma from Stirling's series at 12 and up (lower arguments shifted with an exact double-double product), the zeta series within 1/4 of 1 and 2, the reflection with the exact pi reduction for negative x; tgamma = exp(lgamma) with the reflection's sign, exact factorials to 22!. All coefficients follow from the definitions (no fitted minimax polynomials). `<complex.h>`: no imaginary types (GCC has none), `I` and `CMPLX*` through `__builtin_complex` (pedantic-clean constants), `__STDC_IEC_60559_COMPLEX__` not claimed although the functions follow the Annex G special cases; Kahan's formulas (csqrt, cacos/casin, catanh on x >= 0, ctanh), the others by rotation, cpow through double-double w log z and the D47/D48 cores. Complex accuracy: at most 4 ulp normwise for double, 2 for float. `<tgmath.h>` through `_Generic`, integers as double, several generic arguments combined by adding typed zeros in the unevaluated controlling expression. libc-test's XSI `signgam` comes from a test-only shim header forced into the math suite (C does not reserve the name). | Coefficients that need no fitting tools keep the work clean-room and the accuracy argument direct; claiming Annex G would promise more than the branch-point accuracy delivers. |
| D50 | **Conformance closure** (P1.17, `docs/research/conformance.md`): `tools/scripts/annexb-audit` generates `test/unit/annexb/annexb.c` from Annex B (pdftotext of `docs/standard/annex-b.pdf`): every in-scope identifier must be a macro or usable in `sizeof`, every function is redeclared with its Annex B prototype (name parenthesised, a mismatch does not compile), pseudo-prototypes get existence checks; out-of-scope headers, decimal floating point, Annex K and the macros defined only when supported are excluded. `test/unit/freestanding` links the freestanding headers' and `<string.h>`'s freestanding functions against `libc.a` alone (no port, no startup), so a seam dependency fails the build. `include/stdc-predef.h` holds the library's conditional feature macros: `__STDC_IEC_559__` and `__STDC_IEC_60559_BFP__` only where `<fenv.h>` is complete (x86_64, Arm with a double-precision FPU; the one `#if`, allowed as a feature macro defined only when supported); the toolchain files pass `-include` for it because GCC skips the implicit pre-include under `-nostdinc`. `docs/implementation-defined.md` answers the J.3/J.4 library checklist. `tools/scripts/size-baseline` records the phase-1 sizes (`docs/size/phase-1.md`). | Generated checks keep the audit complete and repeatable; linking without the port is the only test that proves freestanding use. |
| D51 | **Size measurement** (P2.01, `docs/research/size-measurement.md`; supersedes D50's `tools/scripts/size-baseline`): `tools/scripts/size-report` reads `libc.a` and the ELF section flags directly and records per member the bytes of `text`, `rodata`, `unwind` (`.ARM.exidx/extab`, `.eh_frame`), `data`, `bss`, `tdata` and `tbss`; for the reference programs `test/size/{hello-puts,hello-printf-int,hello-printf-float,malloc-heavy,math-mix}` (linked through `port/linux` with `--gc-sections`) the same classes split by origin (program, libc, port, libgcc, other) from the link map, merged input sections scaled to the output; and the frame of every function plus the worst stack depth of 43 entry points and each program's `main` from GCC's `-fcallgraph-info=su` graphs, now compiled into every object with `-fstack-usage` (static calls resolve within the unit first, the largest of several definitions counts; indirect calls, recursion, unbounded dynamic frames and unknown callees mark a lower bound, graph-less assembly and libgcc helpers are named). `report` writes `docs/size/baseline-<arch>.json` from a release build; `diff` compares a build with it and exits 1 when a member, an image or an entry point's stack depth grew (each step justifies growth in `docs/size/<arch>.md`). `compare` builds the reference programs against picolibc 1.8.6 and newlib-nano 4.4 (Ubuntu 24.04 packages pinned by SHA-256, `-std=gnu23`) for `docs/size/comparison.md`, context only. The x86_64 board compiles with `-muclibc`, because the host GCC otherwise fuses `sin`/`cos` into `sincos`, a name C does not reserve and the library does not define. | One tool for every phase 2 metric, computed from the build products themselves with no extra dependencies; the map is the only source that separates the library from the program and the OS layer; the compiler's own call graph avoids disassembly heuristics. |
| D52 | **Build and structure levers** (P2.02, `docs/research/size-levers.md`): measured per flag with `size-report` (D51). Adopted: `-fno-asynchronous-unwind-tables -fno-unwind-tables` on x86_64 (`.eh_frame` was 28 % of `libc.a`; Arm emits none for C). Kept `-Os`: `-Oz` produces identical Arm code. Rejected: `-fshort-enums` (ABI, D15, no gain), `-mslow-flash-data` (cannot access `thread_local`, +3.9 % elsewhere), `-fno-jump-tables`/`-fno-inline-small-functions`/`-fipa-pta` (below 1 %), and LTO: library plus port fails to link (seam objects dropped before archive extraction), library-only saves 2–18 % per image but ties `libc.a` to one GCC and loses the call graphs (Q13). Pull-in chains are broken with link-time references, as D13/D43 do, never `#if`: the `atexit` table lives with its runner `__libc_atexit_run` (a weak call from `exit`); the signal table lives in `raise.c` and `abort` names `raise` weakly (`#pragma weak`), since without `signal` `SIGABRT` can only have its default action; `__libc_assert_fail` and `asctime` format by hand instead of through `snprintf`; closing the `fopen`/`tmpfile` streams at exit is a destructor of `__libc_file_new` (the only maker of streams), so `flush_at_exit` only flushes; `__libc_stdio_flush_all` names `stdout`/`stderr` weakly. The `asctime` result shrinks to the 26 characters C23 defines (other values are undefined and cut short). Archives are recreated from scratch and `clean` removes them, so member order and image layout no longer depend on build history. An empty `main` drops from 1388 to 506 bytes on armv6-m, `assert` from 9665 to 3245. | Every program paid for exit handlers, signals and the formatter it never used; link-time references remove that cost with no change in behaviour and no conditional compilation. |
| D53 | **stdio and printf flavours** (P2.03, `docs/research/stdio-flavours.md`; realises D13, refines D40): flavours are complete archives built from file selection in `libc/libc.mk`, chosen by linking one instead of `libc.a` (a stub archive linked ahead cannot win once `libc.a`'s formatter pulls its own float member). `libc-nofloat.a` swaps in `libc/stdio/nofloat/` stubs: a floating conversion fails with `EINVAL` in `printf` (D28) and is a matching failure with `EINVAL` in `scanf` (D41); `strtod` is unaffected. `libc-minimal-printf.a` adds `libc/stdio/minimal/`: `%n` and the length modifiers wider than `long` are refused with `EINVAL` in both families, so digits need only `unsigned long` division (no 64-bit libgcc helpers); flags, width and precision stay. The engines expose the replaced parts as one-function units: `__libc_format_argument`, `__libc_format_digits`, `__libc_format_count`, `__libc_scan_store`, `__libc_scan_count`; the length enum is shared (`internal/length.h`). The FILE drops `prev` (36 → 32 bytes; the open-stream list is singly linked); `stdin` and `stdout` have static `BUFSIZ` buffers in their own objects and `__libc_file_setup` names `malloc` weakly, so a program using only the standard streams links no allocator (the 256 bytes move from heap to `.bss`); `setvbuf` with a null buffer keeps the stream's own buffer and ignores the size (7.23.5.6p2), a caller's buffer is tracked by `__LIBC_FILE_USER_BUFFER`. armv6-m `hello-printf-int`: 8521 → 8249 bytes with `libc.a`, 5351 with `libc-nofloat.a`, 4779 with `libc-minimal-printf.a` (armv7e-m: 4271, no libgcc). | Link-time selection with no `#if`; failing loudly beats printing nothing; the minimal tier removes exactly what costs 64-bit arithmetic while keeping formatting complete. |
| D54 | **stdlib, allocator, string and time size** (P2.04, `docs/research/stdlib-time-size.md`): a third allocator, `libc/malloc/tiny` (an implicit free list: the header word holds the stride and a used bit, free clears the bit, allocation walks first fit merging free runs, a used sentinel of stride 0 ends the heap; only the port's first heap region), selectable with `LIBC_MALLOC=tiny` and always shipped as `libc-tinymalloc.a`; 13 % smaller than nano, O(chunks) malloc; allocate-only designs are rejected (7.24.3.3). The library's 64-bit divisions by small constants go through `__libc_udivmod64` (high word by a 32-bit division, then 32 shift-subtract steps; native where registers are 64-bit) and `__libc_floor_divmod64`, so printf, strtol, strtod, strftime, asctime and the calendar no longer link libgcc's 64-bit division (`lldiv`/`imaxdiv` keep it); `strtoint` computes its overflow cutoff once. `strerror` is one array of (number + 1, message) records instead of a switch's pointer table. ctype stays as code and qsort as heapsort (measured). armv7e-m `hello-printf-int` 7981 → 7333 bytes (no libgcc); `strerror` 2395 → 1968. | The 64-bit division helper was the largest hidden cost in every printf image; the rest are measured trade-offs. |
| D55 | **Soft-float helper audit** (P2.05a, `docs/research/libm-softfloat.md`): the library converts 64-bit integers through `__libc_u64_to_double`/`__libc_i64_to_double` and integral doubles back through `__libc_double_to_i64` (out of line; the fpv5-dp layer uses two exact 32-bit VCVTs, plus VRINTM, instead of libgcc's `__aeabi_[u]l2d`, which live in the 888-byte soft adder, and `__aeabi_d2[u]lz`; generic: native conversion, and bit shifts for the reverse; x86_64: CVTTSD2SI). The exception-raising helpers and the special-case results (`__libc_math_invalid[f]`, `__libc_math_divbyzero`, `__libc_math_underflow`) move out of line into `libc/fenv`, where `__libc_fenv_records` (from `fenv-arch.h`) makes them arithmetic where flags exist and constants or empty on the soft variant; `__libc_math_overflow` stays inline. A `nofp` libm layer (armv6-m, armv6s-m) defines `__aeabi_dsub`/`__aeabi_fsub` as addition of the negation (IEEE 754 5.4.1), replacing libgcc's 1444- and 764-byte subtraction objects; the D46 layers may therefore add files, not only replace them. `strtof` converts its ≤2^24 significand from 32 bits. Results: no `__aeabi_d*` reference in armv7e-m-dp `libc.a`; `math-mix` 30024 → 27864 (armv6-m), 13244 → 12326 (armv7e-m-dp, no libgcc). `lrint`/`lround` keep `LONG_MIN` for out-of-range values rather than VCVT's saturation. | Every soft-float helper a libm function linked was a hidden cost larger than the function; the replacements are exact by construction and tested against the compiler's conversions. |
| D56 | **Single-precision libm layer** (P2.05b, `docs/research/libm-float-exact.md`): `libc/math/sp` holds `float` functions computed in `float` only and is listed first in `LIBC_MATH_LAYERS` on every Arm core (armv6-m, armv6s-m, armv7e-m, armv7e-m-dp, armv8-m.main); its `subdir.mk` drops what a later FPU layer provides in an instruction (D46). The layer builds with `-Wdouble-promotion` and a `sp.checked` rule that fails the build if any of its objects references a double soft-float helper or a 64-bit conversion that computes in double (`__aeabi_d*`, `f2d`, `f2[u]lz`, `[u]l2[df]`, `[u]i2d`); `-fsingle-precision-constant` is not used (silent semantic change). P2.05b moves the exact functions: rounding, `fmodf`/`remainderf`/`remquof` (`__libc_remainderf`), `frexpf`/`ilogbf`/`llogbf`/`logbf`/`modff`, `l(l)rintf`/`l(l)roundf` (`__libc_float_to_i64`), `(u)fromfp(x)f` (`__libc_fromfpf`) and `nexttowardf` (compared through widened bits). `crealf`/`cimagf`/`conjf`/`cprojf` read the parts directly on every core. `libc.a` grows where a float function was a wrapper, and a program using both `fmod` and `fmodf` pays both cores; a float-only program no longer links soft double: `float-exact` 8440 → 3908 (armv6-m), 5056 → 2048 (armv7e-m, no libgcc), 4552 → 1612 (armv8-m.main), 2002 → 1688 (armv7e-m-dp). x86_64 keeps the double wrappers. | Float-only programs on single-precision or no-FPU cores are the size target (§1); the layer mechanism keeps it one directory, the symbol check keeps it honest. |
| D57 | **Float-float transcendental functions on the soft-`<fenv.h>` cores** (P2.05c1, `docs/research/libm-float-exp.md`): a second single-precision layer, `libc/math/sp-nearest`, after `sp` in `LIBC_MATH_LAYERS` on armv6-m, armv6s-m, armv7e-m and armv8-m.main; its makefile refuses any `LIBC_FENV` but `soft`, because float-float (TwoSum, Veltkamp/Dekker products, `struct __libc_ff` in `internal/math.h`) is exact only when rounding to nearest, the only direction those cores have (D44); armv7e-m-dp keeps the correctly rounded double wrappers. One base-2 core, `__libc_exp2f_core` (Tang: 2^(j/32) as 32 float pairs, e^r - 1 to r^5 on \|r\| < 0.011, about 2^-40), serves `expf`, `exp2f`, `exp10f` and the m1 forms; `__libc_exp2f_scale` rounds subnormal results once through a ±1 bias. Accuracy for float on these cores is faithful and almost always correctly rounded (max 0.51 ulp, at most a few in 10^5 not correctly rounded), enforced by `test/unit/float_accuracy` (max < 1 ulp and at most 1/1000 not correctly rounded; on x86_64 the layer is compiled into the test and run natively on millions of arguments plus every subnormal-result argument); this supersedes D47's "float correctly rounded" for these cores only. New reference program `math-float` (float-only `math-mix`); images grow until every float function of a program is float-only (P2.05c2–e). | Float programs on cores without double hardware stop paying for soft double; float-float keeps the accuracy close to D47. |
| D58 | **Float-float logarithms** (P2.05c2, `docs/research/libm-float-log.md`): `__libc_log2f_core(x, tail)` in `sp-nearest` (Tang: m in [sqrt(1/2), sqrt 2), c = 1 + j/32 for j in [-9, 13], 1/c as a float and -log2 of that float as a pair, 276 bytes; f = m (1/c) - 1 exact through Dekker and Sterbenz; log(1 + f) to f^7 with -f^2/2 from an exact square) serves `logf`, `log2f`, `log10f`, and through `__libc_log2p1f_core` (series below 2^-6, else 1 + x as an exact pair) `log1pf`, `logp1f`, `log2p1f`, `log10p1f`. Every log measures 0.500 ulp, at most 2 in 10^5 not correctly rounded. `__libc_exp2f_scale` (D57) now takes the subnormal path from the magnitude of the result, for any scale of v. `test/unit/float_accuracy` tightens its bound to at most 1 in 10 000 results not correctly rounded. | Same layer, same test, the accuracy of the D47 cores at float cost. |
| D59 | **Float-float powers and roots** (P2.05c3, `docs/research/libm-float-pow.md`): in `sp-nearest`, `__libc_exp2f_signed` (thresholds, the exp2 core, the sign, one rounding) ends `powf`, `powrf` (z = y log2\|x\| as float-float; \|y\| above 2^31 is out of range), `pownf`, `compoundnf` (n as float-float from three exact 21-bit chunks, `__libc_ff_from_i64`, because the compiler's 64-bit conversion computes in double) and `rootnf` (`__libc_ff_divide`); `rsqrtf` and `cbrtf` are Newton steps with residuals from exact products; `hypotf` sums float-float squares and corrects sqrtf by the exact residual, rounding once on the way back; the `sp` layer gains a correctly rounded `sqrtf` for the cores without VSQRT (integer root plus sticky bit, rounded by one integer-to-float conversion, so in every direction). All within 0.509 ulp, at most 2 in 10^5 not correctly rounded. Two defects fixed on the way: the double `compoundn` (P1.14) was wrong for \|x\| < 2^-60 with large n, and `__libc_exp2f_scale` lost the sign of a negative result rounding to zero. | Every float function of `math-float` but sin/cos is now float-only on the soft-fenv cores. |
| D60 | **Float-float sin, cos, tan and the pi-scaled forms** (P2.05d1, `docs/research/libm-float-trig.md`): in `sp-nearest`, `__libc_rem_pio2f` reduces every \|x\| >= 2^-6 in integers (Payne-Hanek: a 128-bit window of an 8-word 2/pi table times the 24-bit significand, k = the six bits at the point, the fraction normalised to 48 bits and multiplied by pi/32), so there is no Cody-Waite path and no double; `__libc_sinf_core(k, s)` (Tang: sin and cos of j pi/32 as 9 float pairs, short series with the cube and the square exact) serves `sinf`, `cosf` (k + 16), `tanf` (quotient), and the pi-scaled forms after an exact float reduction 32x = K + d with the IEEE 754 9.2.1 zeros, ones and poles returned directly. All 0.500 ulp, at most 1 in 2 million not correctly rounded. | The last double core `math-float` linked is gone. |
| D61 | **Float-float inverse trigonometric functions** (P2.05d2, `docs/research/libm-float-atan.md`): `__libc_atan2f_core` in `sp-nearest` (octant reduction; Tang with atan(k/16) as 17 float pairs, \|u\| <= 1/32, the cube and 1/3 as float-float) serves `atanf`, `atan2f` (special cases directly, y/x rounded once when more than 2^40 apart, else operands scaled to the larger), `asinf`/`acosf` (sqrt((1 - x)(1 + x)) exact through `__libc_ff_sqrt`), and the pi-scaled forms (times 1/pi as float-float, exact cases directly, x/pi below 2^-19). All 0.500 ulp. | Same layer and test as D57-D60. |
| D62 | **Float-float hyperbolic functions** (P2.05d3, `docs/research/libm-float-hyperbolic.md`): in `sp-nearest`, `__libc_expf_pair` gives e^\|x\| from the exp2 core and its reciprocal scaled into the same binade (kept up to 2^20, below 2^-40 of the result beyond), so `sinhf`/`coshf` are one float-float sum and one rounding; `tanhf` is (1 - t)/(1 + t) with t = e^-2\|x\|; `asinhf`, `acoshf` (as log1p((x - 1) + sqrt((x - 1)(x + 1)))) and `atanhf` go through the log2 core on exact float-float arguments; odd Taylor series below 1/4 or 1/8 with the cube exact. All 0.500 ulp. `test/unit/float_accuracy` also fails a maximum error of 0.52 ulp or more (every function measures at most 0.509). | Completes the float trigonometric and hyperbolic functions of D48 on the soft-fenv cores. |
| D63 | **Float-float error functions** (P2.05e1, `docs/research/libm-float-erf.md`): in `sp-nearest`, `__libc_erff_core` sums erf's series with positive terms (2/sqrt(pi)) e^-x^2 sum 2^n x^(2n+1)/(2n+1)!! in float-float (no cancellation; the exact square keeps the term ratio exact) with e^-x^2 from the exp2 core; `erff` is +-1 from 3.92 and 2x/sqrt(pi) rounded once below 2^-19; `erfcf` is 1 - erf below 1 and Laplace's continued fraction (150/x^2 + 10 levels, float-float) to 10.06, scaled once by `__libc_exp2f_scale`. No fitted coefficients (as D49). Both 0.500 ulp. run-unit takes a per-test `<name>.timeout` (300 s for `float_accuracy`). | The definitions keep the work clean-room and need no fitting tools; the continued fraction's cost near 1 is the price of having no table. A longer time limit keeps every sample of the accuracy test. |
| D64 | **Float-float gamma functions** (P2.05e2, `docs/research/libm-float-gamma.md`): in `sp-nearest`, `__libc_lgammaf_core` gives log\|gamma(x)\| as float-float with the sign of gamma: Stirling from 8 (scaled by 2^-64 above 2^60), exact downward shifts to [1.5, 2.5), the zeta series of log gamma(1 + z) on [0.5, 2.5) (plus log1p near 2), lgamma(1 + x) - log\|x\| on (-1/2, 1/2), and the reflection with the sin core below. The reflection cancels at the 14 zeros of lgamma in (-9, -2), so within 2^-8/\|psi(z)\| of each the Taylor series sum psi^(k-1)(z)/k! (x - z)^k (z as three floats, 728 bytes) is used; beyond -9 no float lies that close to a zero. `lgammaf` rounds once; `tgammaf` is +-2^(lgamma/ln 2) through `__libc_exp2f_signed`. Coefficients from the definitions (zeta, Bernoulli, polygamma), as D49. Both 0.500 ulp over every float of the swept ranges. | The negative zeros are where every libm's lgammaf loses accuracy; a small table of expansions, derived from the definition, closes it without double arithmetic. |
| D65 | **Float complex functions on the float-float cores** (P2.05e3a–e3c, `docs/research/libm-float-complex.md`): a `libc/complex/sp-nearest` layer, built where `sp-nearest` is in `LIBC_MATH_LAYERS` (complex.mk `SUBDIRS`, libc.mk, a `subdir.mk` dropping the replaced wrappers), with the libm layer's fenv refusal, `-Wdouble-promotion` and helper check. Shared helpers in the libm layer: `__libc_sincosf_core` (float-float sin/cos of a float-float angle; y and 1 - y^2/2 below 2^-20) and `__libc_exp2f_times` (2^z m rounded once, z clamped, tiny m prescaled). `cabsf`/`cargf` are `hypotf`/`atan2f`; `cexpf` rounds each part once from the exp2 core; `clogf` takes log1p(a^2 + b^2 - 1)/2 from exact squares near \|z\| = 1; `csqrtf` is Kahan's method in float-float with y's exponent applied last; `cpowf` is exp(w log z) in base 2 with float-float L and theta. Measured 0.500 ulp normwise and (cpowf aside) 0.5 ulp per part; `test/unit/complex_float` bounds 0.52 normwise everywhere, and per part plus at most 1/1000 misrounded where the layer is linked. armv7e-m-dp and x86_64 keep the double wrappers. | The float complex functions stop linking double arithmetic on the small cores, and the per-part accuracy is far better than D49's 2-ulp normwise allowance at little cost. |
| D66 | **Float complex trigonometric and hyperbolic functions; the double csinh/ccosh sign fix** (P2.05e3b, `docs/research/libm-float-complex.md`): two more libm `sp-nearest` helpers, `__libc_ff_round_product` (2^e a b rounded once, tiny factors prescaled by 2^64, zero factors giving the signed zero) and `__libc_sinhcoshf_core` (float-float sinh and cosh below 16); `csinhf`/`ccoshf` round each part once from float-float products and use `__libc_exp2f_times` from \|x\| = 16; `ctanhf` is (sinh x cosh x + i sin y cos y)/(sinh^2 x + cos^2 y) in float-float (no cancelling denominator), +-1 and 4 sin y cos y e^-2\|x\| from 16; the trigonometric forms by exact rotation. All 0.500 ulp normwise and 0.5 per part. The double `csinh`/`ccosh` beyond \|x\| = 709 used copysign(..., x) on a part whose sign also depends on cos y (sin y); they now multiply by the sign of x. | One float-float product per part keeps each part correctly rounded, which the plain float formulas (several ulps) do not; the double fix is a correctness bug found by the float layer's reference comparison. |
| D67 | **Float complex inverse functions** (P2.05e3c, `docs/research/libm-float-complex.md`): `cacosf` and `casinhf` by Kahan's formulas with float-float sqrt(1 +- z) (`__libc_csqrtf_core`, tiny operands scaled by 2^100), atan2 and asinh (`__libc_asinhf_core`) of float-float arguments, the asymptotic forms from \|z\| = 2^24 and first-order forms for a part below 2^-60; `catanhf` by log1p (`__libc_log1pf_core`) and atan2 of float-float quotients, first-order terms where a quotient is below 2^-60, Re(1/z) +- i pi/2 from 2^40 (float-float products split by 4097 and must stay below about 2^115); the other three by rotation or from cacosf. All 0.500 ulp normwise and 0.5 per part, none misrounded in 262144. | Same reason as D65/D66; the first-order and asymptotic regions are where a part would otherwise pass through subnormal float-float values. |
| D68 | **Float kernel sharing and table sizes** (P2.05e4, `docs/research/libm-float-kernels.md`): f and d kernels stay separate on the single-precision cores (D57: no double arithmetic in float programs; on armv7e-m-dp and x86_64 the float functions are the double ones); inside the float layer `asinhf` and `clogf` now use the D67 cores. `__libc_ff_expm1_series` forms r^2 exactly and adds r^6/720, which lets the exp2 table shrink from 32 to 8 entries while every exponential gets more accurate; the atan2 table goes from 17 to 9 entries; the lgamma zero expansions stop at c6. The log2 table (23) and the sin table (9 rows) stay: halving them cost 0.526 ulp (compoundnf) or touched the reduction for 64 bytes. 312 bytes of tables less. | Measured trade-offs, each against the full accuracy test; the design budget stays 2^-40 although the test resolves only about 2^-34. |
| D69 | **Hand-written string kernels** (P2.06, `docs/research/string-asm.md`): the arch files export `LIBC_STRING_KERNELS` (`thumb1` on armv6-m/armv6s-m); `libc/string/string.mk` builds `libc/string/${LIBC_STRING_KERNELS}`, `libc/string/subdir.mk` drops the C file a `.S` replaces, and `libc/libc.mk` archives it. The kernels keep the D21 word-at-a-time algorithms (no speed flavour exists before Phase 3, so the size gain must not cost the P1.03 speed) and only beat the compiler on encoding: Thumb-1 `memcpy` 96 → 60, `memmove` 176 → 60 (the forward case tail-calls `memcpy`, which copies strictly forwards), `memset` 72 → 48, `strlen` 60 → 48, `strcmp` 22 → 20 bytes; `setjmp`/`longjmp` were already minimal assembly. `test/unit/string_kernels` checks every alignment and length on every board. | One directory per instruction set rather than per core (both Armv6-M cores share Thumb-1); a variable like `LIBC_MATH_LAYERS` keeps it out of `#if`. |
| D70 | **The Thumb-2 cores use the Thumb-1 string kernels** (P2.06b, `docs/research/string-asm.md`): armv7e-m, armv7e-m-dp and armv8-m.main set `LIBC_STRING_KERNELS := thumb1`; against their compiler output (`memcpy` 80, `memmove` 168, `memset` 68, `strlen` 54, `strcmp` 22 bytes) the kernels measure 60, 60, 48, 44, 22. Thumb-2-only encodings would save 4–6 bytes in total (`b.w` in `memmove`, a high-register `tst` in `strlen`), not a second directory's worth. | One shared kernel directory for every Arm core, aligned-only accesses so `CCR.UNALIGN_TRP` cannot fault them. |
| D71 | **Phase 2 close-out** (P2.07, `docs/size/phase-2.md`, `docs/configuration.md`): the final report compares the end of phase 1 with phase 2 per program and arch and against picolibc/newlib-nano; the printf/puts images are 12–27 % smaller on Arm and the float programs the smallest of the three libraries on the single-precision cores, while `math-mix` (float and double together) grew 11–14 % there (Q15). The size budget is `docs/size/budget.json`: code and RAM of every `test/size` image on the five Arm cores as measured here; `tools/scripts/size-report budget` fails a build over it, with +0 % for the size flavour and N = 10 % for the phase 3 speed flavour. `docs/configuration.md` documents the archives, flavours, per-core layers, allocators, ports and build variables for users. | A budget checked by a tool is the only lock that holds; 10 % leaves room for speed kernels and tables while catching a subsystem doubling. |
| D72 | **Benchmark measurement** (P3.01, `docs/research/benchmarks.md`; answers Q5 for now): `test/bench/` holds a harness (`harness/`: `bench_run(name, units, body, context)`, out-of-line `bench_roi_begin`/`bench_roi_end` markers, an `ARCH_FAMILY` cycle counter) and eight programs (string sweep 15 sizes × 4 offset pairs plus mixes, stdio, strto, malloc with `libc.a` and `libc-tinymalloc.a`, qsort, 31 math functions float and double as throughput and latency, `<threads.h>` uncontended), built for every board and build type and run only by `tools/scripts/bench-runner`. The gated figure on **every** board, x86_64 included, is guest instructions per operation from qemu 8.2.2 built with TCG plugins (`make setup-bench` into `local/qemu-8.2.2`; Ubuntu's `qemu-user` has no `-plugin`) and our plugin `tools/qemu-plugins/bench-roi.c`, which counts between the marker addresses; the empty first region calibrates the markers away. x86_64 also records `rdtsc` cycles per operation (native, pinned, fastest of 15), reported but not gated: on this shared VM they move by ±40 % between runs while counts are byte-identical. Baselines are `docs/perf/baseline-<arch>.json` and `docs/perf/<arch>.md`; `make bench` (release, every board) fails on a case that grew by more than 1 % and 2 instructions, `BENCH_UPDATE=1` records. Hardware cycle counts (DWT on a Cortex-M board through `port/none`) remain open for when a board is attached. | Exact, repeatable and host-independent counts are the only gate that holds on shared machines and catches every algorithmic regression; they miss pipeline effects (`LDM` vs `LDR`, FPU latencies), which P3.05's micro-architectural work must state or measure on hardware. |
| D73 | **The speed flavour; Thumb-2 memory kernels** (P3.02a, `docs/research/string-speed.md`; realises D12, answers Q3, supersedes D12's `arch/<arch>/speed/` layout): the speed flavour is a third build type, `speed` (`tools/makefiles/speed.mk`, in `BUILDS`): `-Os` like release (a whole `-O2` library breaks the +10 % budget on every `test/size` image, `hello-printf-int` +35 %) plus `LIBC_FLAVOUR := speed`, with `LIBC_SPEED_CFLAGS` (`-O2`) for hot modules that opt in; it builds into `build/<board>/speed/` and installs into `images/<board>/speed/`, and `make check`, `make bench` (`docs/perf/baseline-<arch>-speed.json`) and `size-report --build-type speed budget --flavour speed` cover it. The arch files give `LIBC_STRING_KERNELS_size` and `LIBC_STRING_KERNELS_speed`; `LIBC_STRING_KERNELS` (deferred) expands the one named by `LIBC_FLAVOUR`, and a list may name several directories, the first with a function winning (each kernel directory's `subdir.mk`). armv7e-m, armv7e-m-dp and armv8-m.main use `thumb2 thumb1` in speed: `libc/string/thumb2/{memcpy,memmove,memset}.S` rely on Armv7-M/v8-M unaligned LDR/STR/LDRH/STRH (never LDM/STM/LDRD/STRD unaligned): a bit-driven branch-skipped tail below 32 bytes, then destination alignment and 32-byte blocks by `LDMIA`/`STMIA` of eight registers (both aligned) or eight unaligned LDRs and one STMIA; `memmove` tail-calls `memcpy` forwards and mirrors it with `LDMDB`/`STMDB` backwards. On armv7e-m: `memcpy` 1024 B 1313 → 174 instructions aligned and 4124 → 441 misaligned, the small-copy mix 215 → 54, `memset` 1024 B 1054 → 177; 319 cases faster, six 1–2 instructions slower (1–4 byte calls). The kernels were trimmed to 180 + 218 + 108 bytes (short tail encodings, an unaligned-store `memset` head) so every speed-flavour `test/size` image stays within the size budget + 10 % (tightest: `malloc-heavy-tiny` on armv8-m.main, 2034 of 2039 bytes). Systems with `CCR.UNALIGN_TRP` set, or copying Device memory, use the size flavour. | A build type gives the flavour its own tree, install and full test run with no `#if` and no `make clean`; unaligned word access is what the compiler already assumes on these cores and removes the byte loop that dominated misaligned copies. |
| D74 | **Thumb-2 word-at-a-time `strcmp` and `strchr`** (P3.02b, `docs/research/string-speed.md`): in `libc/string/thumb2` (speed flavour of armv7e-m, armv7e-m-dp, armv8-m.main). `strcmp`: 16 bytes through a 4× unrolled byte loop (6 instructions a byte against 8: `CMP #1` clears C at the terminator, an IT-conditional `CMP` sets Z for equal bytes), then bytes to a word boundary and, for equal phases, a word per iteration flagged by `((a - 0x01010101) & ~a & 0x80808080) \| (a ^ b)` (Thumb-2 modified immediates, no registers saved); the exit adds the byte index (REV, CLZ, `lsr #3`) to both pointers and reloads the two bytes. `strchr`: DSP `UADD8`/`SEL` masks for the terminator and the character, merged by a second `SEL`. armv7e-m: `strcmp` 1024 B 8220 → 2160 instructions (6190 when the phases differ), 64 B 540 → 240; `strchr` 1024 B 2874 → 2083, empty 52 → 43; no case slower than the phase 2 or P3.02a baselines. A DSP `strcmp` and a DSP `strlen` were measured first and regressed short strings (the push and the exit outweigh the saving below ~20–40 bytes); the classic-test LDRD `strlen` that did not regress is 10 % faster than the Thumb-1 one at 100 against 44 bytes, which the speed budget of `malloc-heavy` (5–13 bytes left) cannot take, so the speed flavour keeps the Thumb-1 `strlen` (Q16). | Per-case instruction counts make setup and exit costs visible: a fast path only wins where the code before it is already cheaper than the size flavour's, so each kernel starts with a loop that is never slower. |
| D75 | **Armv6-M speed kernels** (P3.02c, `docs/research/string-speed.md`): `libc/string/thumb1-speed` (`LIBC_STRING_KERNELS_speed := thumb1-speed thumb1` on armv6-m and armv6s-m). `memcpy`: equal phases at any size by bytes to a boundary, 16-byte `LDMIA`/`STMIA` of r3–r6, words, bytes; different phases bytes below 24 and from 24 aligned source words merged by shifts, `(w >> 8k) \| (x << (32 - 8k))`, two words per iteration (13 instructions per 8 bytes against 32), never reading a word without a needed byte. `memmove`: forward through `memcpy`; backwards indexed by the bytes left (as the size flavour) with 16-byte blocks at start + index and the same merge from the ends. `memset`: registers saved only from 32 bytes (two `STMIA` per iteration), a 3-instruction word loop. `strcmp`: one index register, 7 instructions a byte against 8. `strlen` stays the size flavour's. armv6-m: `memcpy` 1024 B 1314 → 292 aligned, 4125 → 1867 misaligned, the small-copy mix 223 → 136, `memset` 1024 B 1054 → 169, `strcmp` 1024 B 8221 → 7198; 260 cases faster, none slower; kernels 426 bytes against 188, within the speed budget. | Without unaligned access the misaligned copy is where the time goes; shifting aligned words is the only word-wide way, and keeping the size flavour's indexed loops for the short cases is what keeps every small size at or below its old cost. |
