A rational approximation (FreeBSD) needs a division; AOR's single polynomial is about 1 ulp below 1/2 and 2.7 above. |
| D199 | **Fast `atan` and `atan2` on the soft-double cores in fixed point** (P11.05k1, `docs/research/libm-fast-double.md` §17). P11.05k split into k1 (`atan`, `atan2`: a 64-bit quotient) and k2 (`asin`, `acos`: a 64-bit square root), as P11.04f was. `libc/math/dp-fixed/fast/atan.c` and `atan2.c` over one out-of-line kernel `__libc_dp_fixed_atan(y, x)`, y ≤ x, with the quadrant's offset and the sign applied in fixed point: j = round(16 y/x) from one 32-bit division of the top bits; for j ≥ 1 (exponents at most 6 apart), y and x aligned so that c·x and c·y are exact, N = y − c·x and D = x + c·y exactly, \|t\| = \|N\|/D in Q64 by a 64-bit reciprocal (a 17-bit seed and two Newton steps), atan t = t(1 − u·G(u)) with G of six terms by unsigned Horner's rule in 2^8·u, K = atan(j/16) ± atan\|t\| from a 17-entry Q64 table (136 bytes); for j = 0, t = y/x relative; the offsets π/2 and π in Q62. `atan` is K(\|x\|, 1) or π/2 − K(1, \|x\|), one quotient either way; `atan2`'s special cases and far exponents go to D198's slow path, which now stays in every fast build. Measured: `atan` 0.5059 ulp and `atan2` 0.5093 on 1.4·10^9 samples and 2·10^8 breakpoint-edge pairs. Instructions per call: 6081 → 523 on armv7e-m (speed 7741), 17,848 → 2542 on armv6-m (21,175). | Piecewise polynomials around j/16 would need about 13 terms per piece (1.7 KiB of coefficients); the reduction needs one quotient and five terms. A third Newton step changed the worst case by 0.001 ulp. |
| D200 | **Fast `asin` and `acos` on the soft-double cores in fixed point** (P11.05k2, `docs/research/libm-fast-double.md` §18). `libc/math/dp-fixed/fast/asin.c` and `acos.c` over one out-of-line kernel `__libc_dp_fixed_asin`: up to 1/2, asin \|x\| = \|x\|(1 + W(x²)) relative with z = x² in Q64, acos x = π/2 ∓ it in Q62; above, z = (1 − \|x\|)/2 exactly in integers, s = √z by a new 64-bit square root, asin s = s(1 + W(z)), asin \|x\| = π/2 − 2 asin s and acos x = π − 2 asin s (x < 0) in Q62, acos x = 2 asin s (x > 0) relative. W = z·P(z) by fifteen Q64 coefficients fitted here (2^-65.4), Horner's rule in signed 64 bits (one coefficient is negative). The square root: r ≈ 1/√τ in Q30 from a 24-entry 16-bit seed table (2^-5.09) and three Newton steps, s = τ·r, then two corrections by the exact remainder (t − s²)·r/2, in units of 2^-32 and then 2^-64 (within 2 units). Measured: `asin` 0.5049 ulp and `acos` 0.5039 on 1.4·10^9 samples each, about 0.03–0.07 % not correctly rounded. Instructions per call: `asin` 7141 → 661 on armv7e-m (speed 13,029), 20,137 → 3293 on armv6-m (speed 34,625). | Fourteen terms leave 2^-61.9. One exact-remainder correction from the 32-bit root leaves its own square, 2^-59; the second costs four products. |
| D201 | **Fast hyperbolic functions on the double-FPU cores** (P11.05l, `docs/research/libm-fast-double.md` §19). `libc/math/fast/sinh.c`, `cosh.c`, `tanh.c`, `asinh.c`, `acosh.c` and `atanh.c`, on x86_64 and armv7e-m-dp (the soft-double cores run them on their helpers until P11.05m). sinh, cosh, tanh: one kernel `__libc_fast_sinhcosh` for 0 ≤ a ≤ 22 over D187's reduction and table, e^±a = S±(1 + t± + E ± O) with S± = 2^(±k/64) from the same entries and E, O the even and odd parts of exp's polynomial (O within 2^-60.2 of sinh u − u relatively, E within 2^-57.4 of cosh u − 1), S+ ∓ S− by fast two-sums and (S+ + S−)·u an exact product in the difference; sinh below 1/2 and tanh below 1/4 by the odd series with an exact leading term (`__libc_fast_odd_series`: 1/6 and 1/3 as pairs, Q of five and seven terms fitted here, 2^-63.4 and 2^-61.0); cosh needs no series; tanh is the kernel's quotient with its exact remainder up to 22, ±(1 − tiny) beyond; sinh and cosh beyond 22 are e^\|x\|/2 with exp's tail in its polynomial's last step, overflow above 0x1.633ce8fb9f87dp+9. asinh, acosh, atanh: the logarithm of an exact-enough pair (`__libc_fast_log_pair`, D190's evaluation with lo/hi in the tail and k + 1 for log 2·hi), so no log1p form: near a result of 0 the entry has invc = 1 and the pair is relative; the arguments x + √(x² ± 1) with x² exact and the root's remainder (`__libc_fast_sqrt_pair`), (1 + x)/(1 − x) with the quotient's remainder; log\|x\| + ln 2 from 2^28. Measured: at most 0.5493 ulp (`sinh`, `cosh` above 22, exp's own error; 0.537 below), `tanh` 0.5215, `asinh` 0.5151, `acosh` 0.5166, `atanh` 0.5161 on 1.4·10^9 samples each, the SSE2 and fma paths bit for bit. Instructions per call on armv7e-m-dp: `sinh` 200 → 107, `cosh` 192 → 79, `tanh` 202 → 137, `asinh` 287 → 107, `acosh` 246 → 101, `atanh` 217 → 90. | Rounded with the rest, the series' x³ term cost 0.61 ulp (sinh) and 0.56 (tanh), the kernel's (S+ + S−)·u 0.537 and 0.556 just above the series' ends, and adding exp's tail apart 0.5503. A log1p form (FreeBSD's) would need log1p of a pair; the pair's logarithm through the table is relative already. |
| D202 | **Fast `sinh`, `cosh` and `tanh` on the soft-double cores in fixed point** (P11.05m1, `docs/research/libm-fast-double.md` §20). P11.05m split into m1 (these, over the exponential's kernel) and m2 (`asinh`, `acosh`, `atanh`, over the logarithm's and the square root), as P11.04g was. `libc/math/dp-fixed/fast/sinh.c`, `cosh.c` and `tanh.c`, shadowing D201's double files: e^a and e^-a on one integer scale (`__libc_dp_fixed_exp_pair`: t = a·log2 e = n + f by D188's 128-bit product, Y1 = 2^f and Y2 = 2^g for −t = n' + g by D188's kernel fraction, e^-a = 2^(n−63)·(Y2 >> (2n + [f ≠ 0]))), so sinh from 1/4 is (Y1 − Y2')·2^(n−1) and cosh from 2^-27 (Y1 + Y2')·2^(n−1), exact in integers and rounded once, on one path to the overflow threshold. tanh from 1/8 is (Y − c)/(Y + c) with e^(2\|x\|) = 2^(n−63)·Y and c = 2^(63−n), the quotient by D199's 64-bit reciprocal, now `__libc_dp_fixed_reciprocal` in `dp-fixed-fast.h` (the atan kernel unchanged). Below 1/4 (sinh) and 1/8 (tanh), x·(1 ± V) by Q64 series in w = 16x² (six terms) and 64x² (nine, alternating, unsigned Horner's rule subtracting), relative however small x is. Measured: `sinh` 0.5044 ulp, `cosh` 0.5029, `tanh` 0.5083 on 1.8·10^9 samples. Instructions per call: `tanh` 7193 → 500 on armv7e-m and 20,152 → 2534 on armv6-m (speed). | Two exponentials cost no division (Armv6-M has none); e^-a = 1/e^a would. tanh's one exponential of 2\|x\| and a reciprocal is cheaper than two exponentials and the same quotient. |
| D203 | **Fast `asinh`, `acosh` and `atanh` on the soft-double cores in fixed point** (P11.05m2, `docs/research/libm-fast-double.md` §21). `libc/math/dp-fixed/fast/asinh.c`, `acosh.c` and `atanh.c`, shadowing D201's double files. log1p's argument w, or log's argument v where the result is at least 0.88, formed as a 64-bit fixed-point value to about 2^-62 relatively, then two new inlines over D192's kernels: `__libc_dp_fixed_log1p_scaled` (the relative series below 2^-5, else 1 + w in Q63 with the shifted-out bits rounded) and `__libc_dp_fixed_log_scaled` (the table path from 128 bits). asinh: w = \|x\| + x²/(1 + √(1 + x²)) below 1, v = \|x\| + √(x² + 1) from 1, log 2\|x\| from 2^28; acosh: w = t + √(t(2 + t)) with t = x − 1 exact below 2, v = x + √(x² − 1) from 2, log 2x from 2^28; atanh: log1p(2\|x\|/(1 − \|x\|))/2, 1 − \|x\| in Q64, the quotient by D199's reciprocal, the halving in the exponent. D200's 64-bit square root moves out of the asin kernel into `__libc_dp_fixed_root.c`, shared (`asin` and `acos` within 1.5 instructions). Measured: `asinh` 0.5229 ulp, `acosh` 0.5215, `atanh` 0.5229 on 1.4·10^9 samples each. Instructions per call: `asinh` 15,215 → 535 on armv7e-m and 39,784 → 2415 on armv6-m (speed). | Truncating 1 + w, as `log1p`'s 53-bit argument allows, cost 0.533 ulp with a 64-bit w (2 units of 2^-64 just above 2^-5); rounded, 0.523. A Q64 series below 1/4 instead of the table path would need about 15 terms for atanh. |
| D204 | **Fast `erf` on the double-FPU cores** (P11.05n, `docs/research/libm-fast-double.md` §22). `libc/math/fast/erf.c`, on x86_64 and armv7e-m-dp (the soft-double cores run it on their helpers until P11.05o). No exponential: from 1/2 erf is at least 0.52, so 23 pieces of width 1/4 about m = round(4\|x\|)/4 (the 1.5·2^52 shift) approximate erf itself to 2^-59.24, c0 + c1·t + t²·Q(t) of degree 11 with c0 and c1 as pairs and c0 + c1·t formed exactly (two FMAs, c0 − hi exact by Sterbenz; Dekker and a fast two-sum without an FMA); below 1/2, x·2/√π + x³·p0 + x⁵·Q(x²) with the leading two terms exact (2/√π and p0 as pairs, Q of nine terms, 2^-61.9), out of line; below 2^-60 the strict file's scaled product; ±1 from 6. `__libc_fast_product`, an always-inline exact product in `math-fast.h`: at -Os `__libc_dd_product` stays out of line (121 instructions with the calls). Measured: at most 0.5264 ulp on 1.4·10^9 samples per path, the SSE2 and fma paths bit for bit. Instructions per call: 418 → 68 on armv7e-m-dp (AOR 69), 504 → 98 on x86_64; on the soft-double cores' helpers 12,171 → 3206 on armv7e-m (AOR 8263) and 40,655 → 9579 on armv6-m (AOR 25,491). | The series' x³·p0 rounded with the rest cost 0.76 ulp; the strict file's tiny path from 2^-28 drops 0.376·x² of the result (0.53 ulp). A product and a fast two-sum for c0 + c1·t, the Horner loop as written and the float comparisons left 81 instructions, above AOR's 69. |
| D205 | **Fast `erf` on the soft-double cores in fixed point** (P11.05o, `docs/research/libm-fast-double.md` §23). `libc/math/dp-fixed/fast/erf.c`, shadowing D204's double file. From 1/2 to 6, 22 left-aligned pieces of width 1/4: 4\|x\| = significand·2^(e − 50) gives the piece and u in Q64 exactly from x's bits, and erf(a + u/4) = b0 + u·Q(u) with b0 = erf(a) in Q64 and Q's twelve coefficients signed in Q63, fitted here (2^-61.94), by signed Horner's rule; the sum, at least 0.52, rounded once. Below 1/2, x·K(4x²) with K = 2/√π − w·G(w) in Q62, G of nine signed Q64 coefficients (2^-63 relative). Below 2^-60 the strict file's scaled product, ±(1 − tiny) from 6. Measured: at most 0.5044 ulp on 1.4·10^9 samples. Instructions per call: 12,171 → 518 on armv7e-m (AOR 8263, D204's double file on the helpers 3206) and 40,655 → 2049 on armv6-m (AOR 25,491). | Twelve coefficients per piece leave 2^-59.17. |
| D206 | **The fast flavour's comparison with picolibc and newlib-nano leaves out the C23 functions they lack** (P11.06a, `docs/perf/phase-11.md`). picolibc 1.8.6 and newlib-nano 4.4.0 have no `exp2m1`, `exp10m1`, `log2p1`, `log10p1`, `sinpi`, `cospi` or `tanpi` (double or float), which the math benchmark has timed since P11.03d and P11.04a, so `bench-runner compare` no longer built. The comparison builds compile `tools/bench-compare/math.c` (the declarations of `c23-math.h`, then `test/bench/math/math.c` unchanged) and link the placeholders of `c23-math.c`, and `COMPARE_ABSENT` drops those 28 cases from both libraries' results, so no placeholder figure reaches the table or the means (132 shared math cases). `aor-accuracy arm` gains the fast build as a fourth variant, and `bench-runner --build-type fast aor --record` writes `docs/perf/aor-<arch>-fast.json`. | The benchmark source stays the one rtoskit's builds run; the placeholders are never measured. |

---

## 3. Repository layout (target)

```
CLAUDE.md
Makefile                  top-level: BOARDS x BUILDS loop, module ordering (D22)
tools/                    build system (extend, don't restructure)
  makefiles/<board>.mk    BOARD_TYPE: exports ARCH_CROSS and RTOS_PORT (linux-x86_64, ...)
  makefiles/<arch>.mk     ARCH_CROSS toolchain + flags (x86_64, armv6-m, armv6s-m, armv7e-m, armv7e-m-dp, armv8-m.main;
                          the Arm ones include arm-none-eabi.mk)
include/                  public headers (no .mk)
  rtos/rtos-port.h        the RTOS seam contract (installed for port authors)
libc/libc.mk              tree node; archives its children into libc.a (+ libm.a)
libc/<area>/              one module per area, one function per file
libc/<area>/<arch>/       ARCH_CROSS variant subdirectory, selected with SUBDIRS (setjmp, fenv)
libc/<area>/<family>/     ARCH_FAMILY variant subdirectory shared by a triple's cores (tls/arm, D34)
libc/internal/            private headers (never installed, no .mk)
port/linux/               RTOS seam over Linux syscalls (test port) + port/linux/<family>/ variants (x86_64, arm)
port/none/                single-thread, bare-metal default port (no-op locks, one static TLS block)
test/unit/                the unit and conformance tests (D126): api, conformance, libm_vectors, C23 additions, threads.h, seam
test/bench/               Phase 3 benchmarks
toolchain/crosstool-ng/   issue #65: crosstool-NG libc backend, sample configs, patches
docs/standard/            C23 standard text (N3220 clause 7, Annex B)
docs/plan/                this plan and the phase task lists
docs/research/            per-algorithm research notes (D2)
docs/size/, docs/perf/    baselines and reports (Phase 2, Phase 3)
example/                  existing example
```

Each source directory has a `<dir>.mk` made from `tools/templates/` (use
`tools/scripts/mksrc <path>`). See D22 for variants, archives and linking.

---

## 4. The RTOS seam (contract sketch)

The exact signatures are finalised in step P1.01 (bootstrap), P1.04 (heap, locks), P1.09 (I/O), P1.11 (time) and P1.12 (threads).
All the functions are `extern`, have the prefix `__rtos_`, and are implemented by
exactly one port.

| Group | Functions (draft) | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| Process | `__rtos_exit(int)` `[[noreturn]]`, `__rtos_abort(void)` `[[noreturn]]` | `exit`, `_Exit`, `quick_exit`, `abort` |
| TLS | the port calls `__libc_tls_init()`; the port provides the thread pointer (`__aeabi_read_tp` on ARM, `%fs` on x86_64) | `thread_local` |
| Locks | `__rtos_lock_acquire/release(struct __rtos_lock *)` (recursive, opaque port type), `__rtos_lock_create/destroy`, and statically allocated named locks the port defines (`__rtos_malloc_lock`, `__rtos_atexit_lock`, `__rtos_stdin/stdout/stderr_lock`, `__rtos_stdio_lock`; more join with their steps) — final in P1.04 (D31) | malloc, stdio, atexit, `getenv`, time zone state |
| Threads | `__rtos_thread_create(thread, attr, entry, arg)` (a `struct __rtos_thread_attr`: stack size, a caller's stack, policy, priority, inherit; a `void *` result through `exit`/`join`, P10.02), `__rtos_thread_exit/join/detach/self/yield/sleep` (opaque `struct __rtos_thread`), `__rtos_sched_priority_range`, `__rtos_thread_get_sched/set_sched` (D149), `__rtos_futex_wait(address, expected, base, deadline)` (D150)/`__rtos_futex_wake`, and for the `__atomic_*` library `__rtos_atomic_cas` and `__rtos_atomic_enter/exit` — final in P1.12 (D43) | `<threads.h>`, `<stdatomic.h>` library calls |
| Time | `__rtos_clock_gettime(int base, struct timespec *)`, `__rtos_clock_getres(int base, struct timespec *)` (`clock` uses `TIME_ACTIVE`, D42) | `time`, `timespec_get(res)`, `clock`, `thrd_sleep` |
| I/O | `__rtos_read/write(int handle, ...)`, `int __rtos_open(const char *, unsigned int flags)` (`__RTOS_OPEN_READ/WRITE/APPEND/CREATE/TRUNCATE/EXCLUSIVE/TEMPORARY`), `__rtos_close`, `long long __rtos_seek(int, long long, int whence)`, `__rtos_remove`, `__rtos_rename`, `__rtos_tmpname(char *, size_t, unsigned int index)`; negated errno on failure; a port with no file system implements only the std streams and returns `-ENOSYS` — final in P1.09 (D40) | `<stdio.h>` |
| Heap | `bool __rtos_heap_region(unsigned int index, void **base, size_t *size)`, called for index 0, 1, ... until false — final in P1.04 (D31) | `malloc` |
| Env | `char *__rtos_getenv(const char *)`, `int __rtos_system(const char *)` — final in P1.05 (D33) | `getenv`, `system` |

`port/none` must link and run single-threaded with stdout going to a
user-replaceable byte sink. That way a bare-metal image works with no RTOS at all.

---

## 5. Test strategy

- **In-tree conformance tests** (D126) are the main gate, in `test/unit`, run
  by `make check` on every architecture and build type (under
  `qemu-arm -cpu max` on Arm, D36):
  - `test/unit/api` (D122): the header APIs from N3220 clause 7, and every
    public header compiled alone with `-std=c23 -pedantic-errors -Werror`;
  - `test/unit/conformance` (D123): functional cases and one
    `regression_<bug class>` case per bug;
  - `test/unit/libm_vectors` (D124): every libm function in float, double and
    long double, in every rounding direction the core has, with the exception
    flags, against vectors that `tools/scripts/libm-vectors` computes with MPFR.
  - Until D126, musl's libc-test (pinned at `7b95dfa5`, through a manifest, a
    POSIX shim and per-architecture expected results, D27) was this gate; all
    219 applicable tests passed on every architecture when it was removed.
    `docs/research/conformance.md` and `docs/research/libm-vectors.md` map
    each of its tests to the replacement.
- **test/unit** also holds the tests for the C23 additions
  (`<stdbit.h>`, `<stdckdint.h>`, `memset_explicit`, `strfromd`, `timegm`,
  `free_sized`, ...), `<threads.h>`, per-thread `errno` and state, the seam
  contract, and `port/none`.
- **Benchmarks** (phase 3, D72): `test/bench/` programs measured by
  `tools/scripts/bench-runner` under an instruction-counting qemu; `make bench`
  compares with `docs/perf/baseline-<arch>.json` and fails on regressions.
- **Header audit** (P1.17): a generated check that every identifier and macro in
  Annex B is declared with the right type, and that every `__STDC_VERSION_*_H__` is
  `202311L`.
- **qemu-user caveat:** `qemu-arm -cpu max` runs M-profile *instruction subsets* on an
  A-profile CPU model (D36). That is fine for libc code (no system registers), but
  ports must not use M-profile-only instructions in the Linux test port, and qemu
  does not reject instructions outside the selected architecture (the compiler and
  assembler `-march` do). An optional semihosted `qemu-system-arm` (mps2-an385/an505)
  gate can be added later.

---

## 6. Session workflow

See CLAUDE.md. In short: pick the first `[ ]` step in the active phase file,
mark it `[~]`, do the research notes, implement, run `make check` for every
enabled board, update the manifest and the baseline, tick the boxes, append any
decisions here, and commit.

## 7. Open questions

| ID | Question | Default until answered |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Accept D7 (`-mlong-double-64` on x86_64)? | Yes |
| Q2 | ~~libc-test cannot be fetched (`repo.or.cz` denied).~~ **Resolved 2026-10-18:** the network policy now allows `repo.or.cz`, and the submodule is checked out at the pinned commit. | — |
| Q3 | ~~Should size and speed be separate library flavours (D12), or one library tuned for size with speed only where it costs nothing?~~ **Resolved by D73:** separate flavours, the speed one a build type. | — |
| Q4 | What does the rtoskit RTOS port need (is a repo available, which primitives, TLS handling on Cortex-M)? It would be a `port/rtoskit` step after P1.12. | Not planned yet |
| Q5 | Phase 3 measurement on ARM: qemu instruction counts (TCG plugin) only, or also real hardware cycle counts (RP2040 / RP2350 with the DWT cycle counter)? **Partly resolved by D72:** qemu instruction counts through our own plugin on every board (x86_64 too) are the gate; hardware cycle counts are still wanted when a board is attached. | qemu instruction counts, plus hardware if a board is attached |
| Q6 | ~~Time zone support for `localtime`/`mktime`: UTC only, or the POSIX `TZ` string from `getenv("TZ")`?~~ **Resolved by D42:** the POSIX `TZ` string, read at every conversion; no tzdata files. | — |
| Q7 | ~~Is extending `tools/makefiles` with new files acceptable?~~ **Resolved by D22:** follow the pi-pico conventions, and add only board and `ARCH_CROSS` toolchain files there. | — |
| Q8 | ~~Must the crosstool-NG toolchain (issue #65) include C++ (`libstdc++`)? `libstdc++` expects `<wchar.h>`/`<wctype.h>`, which are out of scope, so it would need `--disable-wchar_t`, a C-only toolchain, or a scope change.~~ **Resolved in part by D120 and D121 (issue #98):** yes, without wide characters (`--disable-wchar_t`). The C library side is done: the headers compile as C++, and the C++ ABI termination hooks are in `libc.a`. The toolchain half is tracked in rtoskit-gcc: building `g++` and a hosted, narrow libstdc++ against rtoskit-libc ([sgstreet/rtoskit-gcc#7](https://github.com/sgstreet/rtoskit-gcc/issues/7); a function its configure finds missing comes back here as a decision), and the thread model, `--enable-threads=rtoskit` over `<threads.h>` ([sgstreet/rtoskit-gcc#2](https://github.com/sgstreet/rtoskit-gcc/issues/2)). | Resolved for the library (D120, D121); the toolchain in rtoskit-gcc#7 and #2 |
| Q9 | "cross-ng" is taken to mean **crosstool-NG**. Where does the integration live: patches in this repo (`toolchain/crosstool-ng/`), a crosstool-NG fork, or the existing `red-rocket-computing/cross-dev` toolchain build that `tools/makefiles/setup.mk` downloads from? Which multilib set: GCC's `rmprofile` list, or a custom list limited to the ARM arches in the target matrix? | Patches in this repo; custom multilib list (v6-m, v7e-m+fp, v7e-m+fp.dp, v8-m.main+fp, plus their soft-float variants) |
| Q10 | ~~Beyond the Cortex-M7 DP unit, should the Armv8.1-M cores with an optional DP FPU and MVE (Cortex-M55/M85, `armv8.1-m.main+fp.dp+mve.fp`) be a target too? With D17 that would add an `armv8.1-m.main` layer and reuse `fpv5-dp`.~~ **Resolved by D118:** yes, in Phase 6 (P6.07), with the MVE variants. | Resolved (D118) |
| Q11 | When Clang is enabled (D9), `<stdbit.h>` needs a path for compilers without `__builtin_stdc_*` (Clang 19 has `__builtin_clzg`/`ctzg`/`popcountg` only). A conditional on the compiler is outside the allowed `#if` uses: accept an exception, require a Clang with the builtins, or write the generic macros with `_Generic` over the typed functions? | GCC only; decide when Clang is enabled |
| Q12 | ~~GCC 15.2 does not implement `_BitInt` for 32-bit Arm.~~ **Resolved by D37:** the Arm toolchain is GCC 16.2, which implements it (`__BITINT_MAXWIDTH__` 65535); `test/unit/bitint` runs on every board. | — |
| Q13 | LTO on `libc.a` alone saves 2–18 % per reference image (P2.02), but an LTO archive is bound to one GCC version and loses the `-fcallgraph-info` stack data. When the crosstool-NG toolchain (issue #65) ships `libc.a` with its own compiler, should the size flavour be built with `-flto -ffat-lto-objects` (and the stack report taken from the fat objects)? | Revisit with issue #65 |
| Q14 | P2.05a (D55) left libgcc's soft-float comparisons and division in place on armv6-m (`math-mix` still links 7.7 KB of libgcc there) and pointed at P2.06; P2.06 kept to the string kernels its step lists. Should a hand-written Thumb-1 soft-float set (compare, divide, perhaps add/multiply) replace libgcc's for size, as a separate step with its own exhaustive tests against the compiler's operations? **Partly resolved by D85:** multiplication and division are replaced (smaller and faster). An integer addition was exact but slower than libgcc's, so libgcc keeps it. The comparisons are still open. | Comparisons: revisit with P3.05f–h |
| Q15 | A program that calls float and double transcendental functions together links both kernel sets on the soft-fenv cores (`math-mix` +11–14 % since phase 1, D57). Should a flavour archive (`libc-float-wrappers.a`) offer the float functions as double wrappers, as before P2.05, for such programs? | Revisit in Phase 3 |
| Q16 | ~~After P3.02a the speed flavour's `malloc-heavy` images have 5–13 bytes of their +10 % budget left (D73, D74), so no further speed kernel can reach `memcpy`, `memset` or `strlen` there (a 10 %-faster `strlen` needed +56 bytes). Should the budget be per flavour total rather than per image, should tiny images get an absolute allowance (e.g. +10 % or +256 bytes), or is the limit right as it is?~~ **Resolved by D111:** an absolute floor of 256 bytes of code under the speed margin. | Resolved (D111) |
| Q17 | ~~`qsort` is a heapsort (D33), and `bench-runner compare` counts about 2.6 times the instructions of picolibc's and newlib's `qsort` (P3.06). Should the speed flavour carry an introsort or a merge sort with insertion sort for small runs, and at what code-size cost?~~ **Resolved by D90:** an introsort with a run check in the speed flavour, as fast as picolibc and newlib-nano on random input and faster on ordered input. | — |
| Q18 | The double `exp`, `exp2`, `exp10`, `log`, `log2` and `pow` take 2.2–6.3 times AOR's instructions on every core (P4.03), because every argument goes through the double-double cores (D47). Should they get a double-precision first pass with a proven error bound and a Ziv rounding test, falling back to the double-double core only when the test fails, as D86–D88 did for float on Armv6-M? (`docs/research/optimized-routines.md`, Analysis 1) | Not scheduled; a candidate step |
| Q19 | ~~Our float functions misround 8 arguments, the same 8 on every core and flavour (P4.05, P4.06): `exp2f` 0x1.853a6ep-9 and -0x1.e7526ep-6; `logf` 0x1.b121a6p+76, 0x1.6351d8p+95 and 0x1.827a74p-7; `log10f` 0x1.fddcf4p-98; `cosf` ±0x1.2b9622p+67. This contradicts the correct rounding D47/D57 claim. Find the shared cause, fix it, add these vectors to `test/unit/libm_dense`, and keep an exhaustive host run (`tools/scripts/aor-accuracy`) as every float function's check?~~ **Resolved by D98:** the exhaustive search over every unary float function on the double path found 49 such arguments in 18 functions, including these 8. All are now correctly rounded in every direction on x86_64 and armv7e-m-dp. The float-float layer on the soft-float cores misses these and more within D57's allowance (Q26). | — |
| Q20 | Where the FPU has double, and on x86_64, our float functions call the double cores and take 3–7 times AOR's instructions and 1.6–6.7 KB more code per function (P4.03, P4.04). Should these cores get float kernels that evaluate in double with an error bound and a rounding test, smaller and faster and still correctly rounded? (Analysis 3) | Not scheduled; a candidate step |
| Q21 | ~~Thumb-2 `memchr` is generic C in both flavours and takes about twice AOR's instructions from 16 bytes up (P4.03). A word-at-a-time DSP kernel, like our `strchr` (D74), would close it, but printf links `memchr` and `hello-printf-int-nofloat` has only 93–97 bytes of speed budget left. Decide together with Q16.~~ **Resolved by D113:** a Thumb-2 word-at-a-time `memchr` (0.57–0.75 of the C, 154 bytes) fits the speed budget on every Thumb-2 core. | Resolved (D113) |
| Q22 | Armv6-M `strcmp` takes 3.4 times AOR's word loop at 1 KiB (P4.03). Should the speed flavour carry a word-at-a-time Thumb-1 `strcmp` that keeps the byte loop for short or unaligned strings? No reference image links `strcmp`. | Not scheduled; a candidate step |
| Q23 | On Armv6-M, AOR's hand-written Thumb-1 `fadd`, `fsub` and `fmul` take 0.52–0.61 of the instructions of ours and libgcc's (P4.03), although they carry no `<fenv.h>` contract. Should we write Thumb-1 assembly versions that keep the soft-fenv contract with a round-to-nearest fast path? The comparisons are Q14. | Not scheduled; revisit with Q14 |
| Q24 | ~~On armv7e-m and armv8-m.main every double operation and 64-bit conversion is libgcc's. Should libc provide its own Thumb-2 `__aeabi_d*` and conversion helpers, as D85 did for Armv6-M?~~ **Resolved for armv7e-m and armv8-m.main by D99** (the `soft-dp` layer; it also fixes a misrounding in libgcc's addition). Still open: on armv7e-m-dp only the 64-bit conversions are calls, and they are still libgcc's; `ddiv` (174 instructions) remains behind AOR's 82 (the comparisons, 28.9 after D101, against AOR's 20.2). | Partly resolved (D99); armv7e-m-dp's 64-bit conversions open |
| Q25 | ~~The Armv6-M speed flavour's `log10f` and `powf` have no fixed-point path (D86–D88 stopped short of them) and take 4–4.5 times AOR's instructions (P4.03). `math-float` links `powf` and has only 108 bytes of speed budget left on armv6-m. Should they get the same treatment, and with which budget (cf. D87)?~~ **Resolved by D114** (`log10f`), **D115** (`powf`) and **D116** (the budget: locked speed figures on armv6-m, armv6s-m, armv7-m). | Resolved (D114–D116) |
| Q26 | The float-float layer (`sp-nearest`, D57) that armv6-m, armv6s-m, armv7e-m and armv8-m.main use is below 1 ulp but not correctly rounded. Compiled on the host and searched exhaustively, its `exp2f` misrounds 818 of 2^32 arguments: about 2·10^-7 of them, well inside D57's limit of 1/1000, but a gap to the double path, which is now correctly rounded (D98). Should the layer reach correct rounding? Its float-float precision (about 2^-48) leaves too many hard cases for D98's tables, so it would need a rounding test with a more precise fallback, or a tighter last step, within the soft cores' size budgets. | Not scheduled; D57's allowance stands |
| Q27 | The `__atomic_*` library functions (D43) are atomic only as far as the port's `__rtos_atomic_cas` and `__rtos_atomic_enter/exit` make them, and the seam contract (`include/rtos/rtos-port.h`) only speaks of one core: `port/none` masks interrupts. On armv7e-m and armv8-m.main the compiler inlines the 1-, 2- and 4-byte atomics as `ldrex`/`strex` (`ldaex`/`stlex` on v8-M), which are safe across cores, but every 8-byte atomic calls the library (M-profile has no `ldrexd`), and on armv6-m every atomic does. On a dual-core part such as the RP2350, interrupt masking alone lets the two cores interleave inside the critical section. Should the seam contract require SMP ports to make `__rtos_atomic_enter/exit` exclude the other cores too (a hardware spinlock taken with interrupts masked, e.g. the RP2350 SIO spinlocks), and `__rtos_atomic_cas` likewise where it is not exclusive-access? On SMP Armv6-M (RP2040) a lock-based compare-and-swap is still not atomic against the plain stores the compiler inlines for `atomic_store` on the other core. Is that a supported configuration, and if so, how is it made safe? Note too that the out-of-line 1/2/4-byte functions never use exclusives, even on cores that have them; that is harmless while the compiler inlines those sizes. | Open: decide the SMP contract before the `rtoskit` port |
| Q28 | ~~armv7-m's speed-flavour `math-float` image is over its budget: D108 locked armv7-m's entry in `docs/size/budget.json` from the size-flavour image (8300 bytes, so 9960 with the speed margin of 20 %), but the speed flavour adds `nofp/speed`'s fixed-point paths. D108 measured 10644 bytes, D109 10536. `size-report budget --flavour speed` was not part of D108's validation. Should armv7-m's speed budget be locked from its speed image, as armv6-m's margin reflects the same paths (D87), or should armv7-m drop some fixed-point paths that Thumb-2 with `UMULL` and the `soft-sp` helpers no longer needs?~~ **Resolved by D110**: armv7-m's speed budget is locked from its speed images; the fixed-point paths stay (they take 0.03–0.27 of the size flavour's instructions there). | Resolved (D110) |
| Q29 | Are the classic Arm cores in scope: Armv4T (ARM7TDMI, ARM9TDMI), Armv5TE (ARM926EJ-S, ARM946E-S) and Armv6/Armv6KZ (ARM1136, ARM1176)? GCC 16.2 still accepts some of these `-march` values; they have no Thumb-2, no `LDREX` before Armv6 (so no lock-free atomics), and VFPv2 at most. P6.01 records what GCC 16.2 and qemu still support. | Out of scope unless P6.01 finds a user; then P6.12 |
| Q30 | AAPCS64 makes `long double` IEEE binary128, and GCC has no `-mlong-double-64` for AArch64, so D7's "binary64 everywhere" cannot hold there. Binary128 needs exact `printf`/`strtold`/`scanf` conversions, `<float.h>` and `<math.h>` limits, the `*l` libm functions and their `<tgmath.h>` forms, and the soft-quad arithmetic helpers (`__addtf3` and the rest). Which accuracy goal do the `*l` functions take, and are the helpers ours or libgcc's (D102 allows libgcc for symbols the library does not define)? | Binary128 with exact conversions; `*l` functions below 1 ulp, correctly rounded where cheap; libgcc's soft-quad arithmetic |
| Q31 | A and R profile AArch32 cores run Arm or Thumb code. Which state does each flavour build in? Thumb-2 is smaller; Arm may be faster on the in-order A and R cores (qemu's instruction count cannot show it, D84). Every `.S` kernel must interwork either way. | Thumb-2 in both flavours; Arm in the speed flavour only where a measurement justifies it |
| Q32 | `jmp_buf` is `10 * sizeof(void *) + 64` bytes (D33), 144 on AArch64, which needs 168 (x19-x30, sp, d8-d15). The headers are per triple (D15), so the expression may change, but `arm-none-eabi` must keep its 104 bytes or accept an ABI change. Revise the expression (e.g. per-pointer-size terms) or give AArch64 its own constant through an `__arch_*.h` piece? | An expression that still gives 104 bytes on 32-bit Arm and 168 on AArch64, checked by `static_assert` in each `setjmp` |
| Q33 | With 15 or more architectures, `make check` (21 configurations today) and `make bench` take several times longer. Should `ARCHS` default to a representative subset, with the full list as a named set for CI and phase gates? | `ARCHS` stays the full list; revisit when a full `make check` exceeds an hour |
| Q34 | Armv8.1-M PACBTI (Cortex-M85) and AArch64 BTI/PAC: should the library build with `-mbranch-protection=standard`? The instructions sit in the hint space (NOPs on cores without them), but they cost code size, and `setjmp`/`longjmp` and the `.S` kernels must carry landing pads and sign return addresses consistently. | Off in the size flavour; decided by measurement in P6.07 and P6.08 |
| Q35 | The A and R profiles allow unaligned `LDR`/`STR` to Normal memory, and GCC assumes it (`-munaligned-access`). Do the AArch32 and AArch64 builds keep that default, which requires the caller's data to be in Normal memory, or build `-mno-unaligned-access` as the Cortex-M kernels' read rules assume (`docs/research/strlen-thumb2.md`)? | GCC's default; kernels still read only aligned words inside the object's granule |

(2) In the directed modes, `asinf`, `tanf`, `atanhf`, `sinhf` and `log1pf` at −FLT_MIN and `tgammaf` at ±FLT_MIN are 1 ulp from the correctly rounded result: the double function's tiny-argument shortcut returns x unchanged, and the conversion to float rounds that exact value. That contradicts the README's "float, directed rounding: correctly rounded". Both are within the documented contracts (D47, D98), so the harness passes. Should the code be fixed (a cbrt with a final correction step; a directed-mode correction on the tiny-argument shortcut), or should the README claims be narrowed?~~ **Resolved by D125:** both fixed, with `tgamma` and `pow` misroundings the same vectors found, and the harness now enforces the README claims. | — |

| Q48 | ~~Does `fast` keep `errno` and floating-point exceptions (D162)? (A) As today: no `errno`, `MATH_ERREXCEPT`, invalid, divide-by-zero, overflow and deserved underflow raised on the cores that have exceptions (x86_64, armv7e-m-dp), inexact unspecified. It costs nothing on the five soft-double cores and a few instructions on rare branches of the two FPU cores, and keeps one header set and Annex F. (B) No exceptions: `math_errhandling` would have to be 0, which C23 7.12p20 forbids in a hosted implementation, and `__STDC_IEC_559__` would go: a header that differs per build type, which D15 and a toolchain's shared `include/` rule out, for a gain of a few instructions on error paths. (C) Add `errno` (`MATH_ERRNO \| MATH_ERREXCEPT`): a TLS access on every error path and also a header change; no flavour sets `errno` today. Recommendation: A (`docs/research/fast-math.md` §4.3).~~ **Resolved by D162:** the maintainer's decision: `fast` keeps `errno` and the floating-point exceptions as the other build types do, on every core; it relaxes only accuracy and rounding (nearest only). | — |
